################################################################################
PR #511 [OPEN, draft] Imbalance-aware radial partitioning
################################################################################
author: jurasic-pf | opened: 2026-05-04 18:26 UTC
branch: proximafusion:impalance-aware-radial-partitioning -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/511
files (4): vmecpp/vmec/radial_partitioning/BUILD.bazel +2 -0, vmecpp/vmec/radial_partitioning/radial_partitioning.cc +80 -11, vmecpp/vmec/radial_partitioning/radial_partitioning_test.cc +147 -22, vmecpp/vmec/vmec_constants/vmec_algorithm_constants.h +27 -0

--- description ---
Boundary threads (0, N-1) have an additional constant per-call overhead beyond the usual per-surface cost:
* degenerate-axis init at j=0
* NESTOR/free-boundary handover at j=ns-1, etc.

Under equal-share partitioning these threads consistently arrive last at every team barrier, and the gap grows with thread count. The result is a scaling cliff: 8-30% of thread-time is wasted at barriers waiting for the boundary threads, and adding more threads yields diminishing returns past ~10 threads.

Reframe the partition as distributing `total_work = num_surfaces + k_axis + k_lcfs` *effective work units* evenly across threads, then convert each thread's assigned work back to a surface count by subtracting the inherent boundary overhead it carries (k_axis surfaces of axis overhead for tid 0, k_lcfs for tid N-1). With Hamilton's method to distribute the integer remainder, the resulting partition is makespan-optimal under the contiguous-slice constraint:

  * At low thread counts the boundary overhead is a small fraction of
    total work, so the partition converges to the equal partition.
  * At high thread counts it shifts a meaningful number of surfaces
    off the boundaries onto the interior, balancing per-thread work.

Both `k_axis` and `k_lcfs` live in `vmec_algorithm_constants.h` as `kBoundarySurfacesOffAxis` (=2) and `kBoundarySurfacesOffLcfs` (=1). They were derived empirically by sweeping wall-clock time across
(NS, num_threads); see VMECPP_AXIS_SURFACES_OFF and VMECPP_LCFS_SURFACES_OFF env vars for runtime override.

PR #512 [OPEN, draft] WIP: partitioning investigation artifacts
################################################################################
author: jurasic-pf | opened: 2026-05-04 18:26 UTC
branch: proximafusion:partitioning-investigation -> graphite-base/512
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/512
files (4): OPTIMIZATION_REPORT.md (added) +324 -0, benchmarks/measure_boundary_overhead.py (added) +232 -0, benchmarks/simulate_partition.py (added) +291 -0, benchmarks/sweep_partition_offsets.py (added) +145 -0

--- description ---
Investigation-only branch carrying the diagnostic scripts and the
running notes that informed the imbalance-aware radial partitioning
work on partition-aware. Intentionally not merged to main.

* OPTIMIZATION_REPORT.md -- running write-up of measurements (baseline,
  per-thread function-body times, sweep tables, conclusions).
* scripts/measure_boundary_overhead.py -- per-thread cumulative time
  inside computeJacobian.body across NS values; fits the additive
  boundary-overhead model.
* scripts/simulate_partition.py -- predicts makespan under both the
  multiplicative and additive partitioning models, useful for tuning
  the kBoundarySurfacesOff* constants.
* scripts/sweep_partition_offsets.py -- A/B wall-clock sweep across
  (axis_off, lcfs_off) combinations, used to verify the partitioner
  empirically.
* examples/data/input.w7x -- bumped NS_ARRAY to 300 and tightened
  FTOL_ARRAY to 1e-9 so the per-iteration savings actually show up
  above the bench noise floor on this longer-running case.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>

PR #526 [OPEN] add docling version of TNOV
################################################################################
author: jons-pf | opened: 2026-05-20 10:18 UTC
branch: proximafusion:docling-tnov -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/526
files (1): docs/the_numerics_of_vmecpp.json (added) +49203 -0

--- description ---
How was this done?

Starting from https://github.com/proximafusion/the_numerics_of_vmecpp :

```bash
latexmk -pdf main.tex

latexpand \
  --fatal \
  --keep-comments \
  --biber main.bbl \
  -o main.flat.tex \
  main.tex

# Make sure no unresolved includes remain.
grep -nE '\\(input|include)\{' main.flat.tex

# Make sure the flattened file is still valid LaTeX.
latexmk -pdf main.flat.tex

# Convert the flattened LaTeX file to Docling JSON.
docling main.flat.tex --from latex --to json --output ./docling_out
```

################################################################################
PR #537 [OPEN] output_quantities: implement lbsubs full-grid B_s via radial force balance
################################################################################
author: CharlesCNorton | opened: 2026-05-28 19:34 UTC
branch: CharlesCNorton:implement-lbsubs -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/537
files (11): README.md +0 -1, src/vmecpp/__init__.py +4 -0, src/vmecpp/_util.py +1 -0, vmecpp/common/vmec_indata/vmec_indata.cc +22 -0, vmecpp/common/vmec_indata/vmec_indata.h +10 -0, vmecpp/common/vmec_indata/vmec_indata_test.cc +3 -0, vmecpp/vmec/output_quantities/BUILD.bazel +20 -0, vmecpp/vmec/output_quantities/lbsubs_test.cc (added) +321 -0, vmecpp/vmec/output_quantities/output_quantities.cc +425 -2, vmecpp/vmec/output_quantities/output_quantities.h +34 -0, vmecpp/vmec/pybind11/pybind_vmec.cc +1 -0

--- description ---
## Problem

`lbsubs` was listed as unimplemented in the README, and `ComputeOutputQuantities` left the full-grid covariant `B_s` as the linear half-to-full interpolation of the metric-element `B_s`, with a standing `TODO` at the call site (`re-compute B_s to solve radial force balance (lbsubs flag in Fortran VMEC)`). The `jxbout` diagnostic that consumes the `B_s` tangential derivatives — `itheta`/`izeta` (the `sqrt(g)` `K^{u,v}` currents) and `bsubs3` — therefore omits the current-sheet correction that the `lbsubs` path captures.

## Change

Add a `VmecINDATA::lbsubs` flag (parsed from JSON, persisted to/from HDF5, round-tripped through `ToJson`; default `false`) and implement `RecomputeBSubSFromRadialForceBalance`, a port of the `IF (lbsubs)` branch of `jxbforce.f90` together with the collocation solve in `getbsubs.f90`. On each interior full-grid surface it solves

```
bsupu * d(B_s)/du + bsupv * d(B_s)/dv = brho
```

in spectral space, where `brho` is the `sqrt(g)`-weighted radial-force residual with the flux-surface-averaged force balance subtracted (the local equation is otherwise unsolvable, since that surface integral vanishes identically). The recovered coefficients overwrite the interior `bsubs_full` and its tangential derivatives `bsubsu`/`bsubsv`; axis and edge are filled by the existing `ExtrapolateBSubS`. The solve is guarded: a singular matrix or a residual above `1e-8 * |rhs|` logs a warning and leaves that surface at its half-to-full interpolation. Both the stellarator-symmetric and the non-stellarator-symmetric (cos-basis) coefficient layouts follow `getbsubs.f90`. With `lbsubs == false` the output path is unchanged.

## Verification

New `lbsubs_test` (gtest), solovev fixture:
- `lbsubs == false` leaves `bsubs_full` equal to `0.5 * (bsubs_half[:, jF] + bsubs_half[:, jF-1])` on every interior surface (max abs diff `< 1e-12`).
- `lbsubs == true` changes `bsubs_full` measurably against the `false` baseline (guards against a silent no-op).
- the recovered `B_s` satisfies `bsupu * dB_s/du + bsupv * dB_s/dv = brho` at every interior collocation point, residual `< 1e-7 * |rhs|`, with the RHS re-derived independently of the implementation's scratch buffers.
- the `lbsubs` JSON flag round-trips.

Local run: 4 passed, 1 skipped. The skipped case parses `up_down_asym.json` (`lasym = true`) and validates input handling but does not run the solve, since the equilibrium iteration does not yet support `lasym = true`; the non-stellarator-symmetric end-to-end path is deferred. README updated to drop `lbsubs` from the unimplemented list.

Wider PARVMEC sweep, in case it's useful. Interior full-grid `B_s`, as max abs difference between the two codes' `lbsubs` corrections relative to the correction magnitude:

| case | ns | nzeta | agreement |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| solovev | 55 | 1 | 2.3e-7 |
| solovev_no_axis | 11 | 1 | 5.3e-10 |
| solovev_analytical | 31 | 1 | 3.6e-8 |
| cth_like_fixed_bdy | 25 | 36 | 1.4e-4 |
| cma | 51 | 16 | 1.4e-1 |
| cth_like_fixed_bdy_nzeta_37 | 25 | 37 | no correction in either code |

cma looks like a difference between the Fortran generations rather than a VMEC++ one: VMEC++ reproduces the 8.52 `jxbout_cma.nc` reference to 3.5e-12, while the PARVMEC 10.0 run differs from that same reference by 3.1e-3, and the collocation solve amplifies it.

cth_like_fixed_bdy_nzeta_37 has odd `nzeta`, so the collocation row count cannot match `itotal` (299 expected, 297 built) and every interior surface falls back to the half-to-full interpolation. PARVMEC reports the same two counts and does the same thing, so `lbsubs` is a no-op for odd `nzeta` in both codes.

# CUDA port of the iteration body (opt-in via VMECPP_USE_CUDA)

## Summary

This PR adds an opt-in CUDA execution path for the iteration body,
covering fixed-boundary and free-boundary configurations. Built with `-DVMECPP_USE_CUDA=ON`, the per-iteration chain (forward
toroidal transform, Jacobian and metric elements, MHD forces, inverse
transform, preconditioning, residuals, time step) executes on the GPU with
device-resident state; `vmecpp.run` and `vmec_standalone` use it
transparently for supported inputs. A batched mode solves N equilibria
concurrently inside one CUDA context, exposed to Python as
`run_batched_gpu`: a broadcast call returns the single converged result of
the seed configuration, and a distinct call returns one `OutputQuantities`
per configuration, derived directly from the batched run's flushed device
state; each carries that configuration's own iteration count and final
force residuals, snapshotted while it was still active so a converged
configuration reports its own residual rather than a post-mask zero. Every
environment variable the binding sets is restored when the call returns.

The option defaults to OFF. Without it, the build requires no CUDA toolkit
and contains no CUDA reference.

The build runs on Linux and natively on Windows with the MSVC host
compiler (system dependencies from vcpkg, the Ninja generator, and a
CUDA compiler launcher that adapts the MSVC command line). All
Windows-specific handling is guarded under `_WIN32`/`MSVC`, so the Linux
build is byte-identical; the native Windows module builds and runs `cma`
on an RTX 6000 Ada to the canonical result. See the Windows section of
the docs for the exact steps.

Documentation: `docs/cuda_acceleration.md` (scope, build, correctness
contract, batched mode, and a reference of every runtime control).
Regression coverage: `tests/test_cuda_gpu.py`, opt-in via
`VMECPP_TEST_CUDA=1` since it requires a CUDA build and a GPU.

## Scope

Supported: fixed-boundary and free-boundary, stellarator-symmetric
(`lasym = false`) configurations, both axisymmetric (`ntor = 0`, the
degenerate `nZeta = 1` case) and three-dimensional (`ntor >= 1`), on a
single radial rank, in both the single and the batched execution modes.
Free-boundary runs keep the NESTOR vacuum solve on the host with
per-iteration bridges to the device-resident iteration state; the
segment and whole-iteration CUDA graphs are disabled for them, since
the edge-force kernel toggles with the vacuum pressure state. Sync
elision covers free-boundary runs: iterations run live until the
vacuum contribution is fully active, then the scalar sync sites elide
while the vacuum block keeps its per-iteration cadence (the NESTOR
response must track the boundary every iteration; a boundary-cadence
vacuum solve was measured non-convergent and rejected). Batched free-boundary runs carry one NESTOR
instance per configuration against one loaded mgrid (every input in the
batch must share `mgrid_file` and `extcur`; boundaries and plasma
profiles may differ in distinct mode); the vacuum activation, the
`nvacskip` cadence, and the soft-start restart are batch-wide decisions,
and the per-iteration vacuum solves serialize on the host across the
configurations, which bounds the batched free-boundary speedup below
the fixed-boundary one.

Enforced at runtime under the CUDA build:

- `Vmec::run` rejects `lasym = true` inputs with
  `absl::UnimplementedError` before any iteration work, along with batched
  free-boundary inputs whose vacuum solver is not NESTOR.
- Inputs with `nThetaReduced > 256` are rejected with
  `absl::UnimplementedError` (a poloidal-scatter kernel-layout limit). The
  radial resolution is accepted up to `CudaMaxRadialResolution()`: the
  parallel cyclic reduction solver carries one thread per radial row through
  1024 rows, and the block-Thomas solver holds its elimination ratios in
  dynamic shared memory sized to `ns` above that, so the device's opt-in
  shared-memory capacity sets the ceiling; an `ns` beyond it is rejected.
- An estimate of the run's device-memory footprint is checked against the
  device's free memory before the first allocation; an oversized batch is
  rejected with `absl::ResourceExhaustedError` instead of failing at an
  allocation deep inside the iteration body.
- CUDA and cuFFT failures surface as recoverable error statuses (a Python
  `RuntimeError` through the bindings) instead of terminating the process;
  the next run re-stages the device state from scratch.
- The radial domain is not partitioned across OpenMP threads;
  `num_threads` is clamped to 1 (host threading offers no benefit when the
  iteration body runs on the device).

## Changes when the option is OFF

The CPU algorithm and its behavior are unchanged. Two things in shared
code are new:

1. `FlowControl` gains per-configuration state vectors (sized 1 and inert
   outside the batched CUDA mode) and a typed `RestartReason` enum with
   documentation of the Fortran `irst` correspondence.
2. `ideal_mhd_model.cc` gains phase-timing instrumentation
   (`phase_timer.h`): an RAII scope timer around each iteration phase that
   writes a summary to a file under `/tmp` (path configurable via
   `VMECPP_PHASE_TIMING_PATH`). The instrumentation is compiled only under
   `VMECPP_USE_CUDA`; in CPU-only builds the macro expands to nothing.

The remaining CPU-side changes are `#ifdef VMECPP_USE_CUDA` dispatch
sites and are compiled out.

## Architecture

- All iteration state (spectral coefficients, geometry, forces,
  preconditioner matrices, velocity and position of the time integrator)
  persists in device buffers across iterations. Host transfers are limited
  to a small number of scalars per iteration (Jacobian tau extrema,
  residual triples, plasma volume), plus consolidated flushes at
  preconditioner-update and end-of-run boundaries.
- The toroidal transforms run as batched cuFFT plans; the poloidal
  transforms are fused into fill/scatter kernels on either side. The
  dealias and scatter kernels warp-pack `32 / nThetaReduced` zeta planes
  onto each 32-lane warp (default on), filling the lanes the one-plane
  layout left idle when the reduced poloidal extent is 16.
- Three sync-free segments of the iteration body are captured as CUDA
  graphs and replayed per iteration (default on, each individually
  defeatable by environment variable).
- The batched mode sizes every per-configuration buffer by
  `VMECPP_N_CONFIG_MAX` and carries a configuration axis on each kernel
  grid. Per-configuration residual evolution, convergence gating,
  time-step control, bad-Jacobian restart, and an active-configuration
  kernel mask let configurations converge independently; a configuration
  that exceeds its iteration cap is marked timed out without failing the
  batch.

## Correctness

Contract, verified on fixed-boundary stellarator configurations through
the full multigrid ramp:

- `aspect_ratio` matches the CPU result bit for bit.
- Volume-averaged and field-line-derived output quantities match within
  1e-5 to 1e-3 relative. The origin is floating-point summation-order
  differences in reductions; each such reduction is documented at its
  kernel.
- Iteration counts, restart-reason sequences, and multigrid stage
  transitions match the CPU trajectory. On inputs that trigger mid-run
  bad-Jacobian restarts, the timing of restart events is sensitive to
  the documented drift, so iteration counts there match approximately
  rather than exactly (W7-X converges in 3146 iterations against the
  CPU's 2954, to the same equilibrium).
- On free-boundary runs the boundary itself responds to the vacuum
  field through the drift-sensitive trajectory, so every converged
  output carries the drift family there; the bit-exact `aspect_ratio`
  clause applies to fixed-boundary runs.

Reductions that feed the time-step controller and the convergence gate use
serial or order-preserving device implementations and match the CPU bit
for bit per call. Reductions covered by the drift tolerance (for example
the differential volume under the fused atomicAdd kernel) are documented
as such.

## Validation

CPU-only build (option off):

- `vmec_standalone` and the Python path reproduce upstream `main` results
  on `cma`; the free-boundary case `cth_like_free_bdy` runs unchanged.
- No instrumentation side effects: a run writes no timing file.

CUDA build, development machine (RTX 6000 Ada Generation, Linux under
WSL2):

- Production stellarator boundary (mpol = 10, ntor = 10, nfp = 3,
  ns = [25, 51, 99]) through the full multigrid ramp: `aspect_ratio`
  bit-identical to the CPU result, derived metrics inside the documented
  drift family.
- `cma`: CPU and CUDA volume and aspect ratio equal to 12 significant
  digits.
- Axisymmetric boundaries (`solovev`, `circular_tokamak`) match the CPU
  build bit for bit, single-grid and through the multigrid ramp; `lasym`
  inputs are rejected with `UnimplementedError`; `vmec_standalone` with
  default thread settings runs single-rank.

CUDA build, second machine (RTX 3070 Ti Laptop, sm_86, 8 GB, CUDA
toolkit 12.0 with gcc-12 host compiler, native Ubuntu 24.04):

- Same battery: convergence trajectories identical to the development
  machine, outputs equal to 12 significant digits.
- The `cma` wout volume and aspect ratio are bit-identical to the
  development machine (hex-level comparison) for the single run, the
  N = 4 broadcast, sync elision, and the whole-iteration graph; the
  duplicated-boundary distinct run is bit-identical across its
  configurations; the direct per-configuration output derivation
  returns the same twelve significant digits for both boundaries; the
  spectral-width progress column matches to the printed digit.
- The fixed-boundary W7-X example (`examples/data/w7x.json`, mpol = 12,
  ntor = 12, nfp = 5, single stage at ns = 99) converges to the CPU
  build's equilibrium: the MHD energy agrees to all seven printed
  digits and the converged axis position and beta match. This input
  exercises the mid-run bad-Jacobian recovery path, which no other
  validation input triggers.
- Hardening coverage on the same battery: the shape guards, the memory
  pre-flight (rejection plus same-process recovery), device selection
  with out-of-range fallback, mixed configuration counts across runs in
  one process, and broadcast at N = 2 and N = 4 bit-identical to the
  single run, including through the iteration-1 magnetic-axis recovery,
  which re-stages the device position state from the recomputed axis.

CUDA build, third machine (NVIDIA A100-SXM4-80GB, sm_80, CUDA toolkit
12.4, Ubuntu 22.04 container):

- Built with the default `CMAKE_CUDA_ARCHITECTURES` list, so this run
  executes the native sm_80 code path of the shipped fat binary on the
  datacenter Ampere die.
- On an 8x A100 node, eight processes pinned one per device each
  completed a 16-configuration batch concurrently, with all eight
  results bit-identical across devices.
- Full battery: `cma` wout volume and aspect ratio bit-identical to both
  other machines (hex-level comparison) for the single run, the N = 4
  broadcast, sync elision, and the whole-iteration graph; the
  duplicated-boundary distinct run is bit-identical across its
  configurations; the direct per-configuration output derivation returns
  the same twelve significant digits for both boundaries; the
  spectral-width progress column matches both machines to the printed
  digit.
- The opt-in test suite passes on a single A100 (eleven cases,
  including both free-boundary forms, the W7-X bad-Jacobian recovery,
  and the tensor-core scatter at W7-X's full poloidal spectrum), and
  the experiment-gate survey passes for all twenty-three gates with
  drift values matching the other machines.

Batched mode, development machine and RTX 3070 Ti:

- `run_batched_gpu` driven directly on `cma`: broadcast at N = 4 and
  distinct at N = 2 complete with the CUDA graphs active and return
  per-configuration outputs equal to the single-configuration run.
- Broadcast at N = 4 is bit-identical to the single-configuration run
  (hex-level wout comparison) with the per-configuration time-step
  controller active.
- Distinct mode with two different boundaries converges each
  configuration to its own equilibrium (the converged spectra differ by
  exactly the imposed boundary ratio); with duplicated boundaries the
  per-configuration states are bit-identical at every probed iteration
  of both multigrid stages.
- Two consecutive distinct-mode `run_batched_gpu` calls in one process
  return identical per-configuration outputs; the second call restages
  its inputs from clean device state and its multigrid stage transition
  replays the first call's bit for bit.
- Free-boundary: the cth-like case converges to the CPU equilibrium in
  both its single-stage and multigrid forms (volume within 1.4e-5 and
  1.0e-5 relative respectively, aspect and MHD energy within 1e-6),
  with the NESTOR consistency checks reporting the same toroidal
  current and poloidal field as the CPU run.
- `tests/test_cuda_gpu.py` pins the above as a repeatable suite: the
  canonical single-configuration result, repeated-run bitwise
  determinism, broadcast returning a single output through the
  iteration-1 axis recovery, bitwise-identical consecutive distinct
  calls, mixed configuration counts in one process, the shape guards
  and memory pre-flight with same-process recovery, sync-elision and
  whole-iteration-graph bit-identity, the Ozaki-3 scatter family
  result, the staged int8 limb descent, both cth-like free-boundary
  forms (single-stage and the multigrid stage transition), both forms
  again under sync elision with a broadcast batch, the
  free-boundary wout fields against a committed reference, a
  free-boundary broadcast against the single run, a two-boundary
  distinct free-boundary batch, a fixed-boundary run followed by a
  free-boundary run in one process, hot restart, the axisymmetric
  `solovev` and `circular_tokamak` boundaries in single, multigrid,
  distinct-batch, and broadcast forms, W7-X convergence through the
  mid-run bad-Jacobian recovery, and W7-X under the wmma
  scatter at its full poloidal spectrum (the W7-X cases are
  additionally gated behind `VMECPP_TEST_CUDA_SLOW=1`).

## Performance

Measured on an RTX 6000 Ada Generation (i9-13900KF host, Linux under
WSL2; native Linux carries less launch overhead), on a production
stellarator boundary with mpol = 10, ntor = 10, nfp = 3, multigrid
ns = [25, 51, 99], approximately 11,500 iterations to convergence:

- Single configuration: about 10 s wall for the full run, roughly
  0.9 ms per iteration averaged over the ramp.
- Batched distinct mode at N = 16 to 32: about 4.3 s per equilibrium.
- For reference, the same equilibrium evaluation through our CPU pipeline
  takes about 54 s per equilibrium on the same machine.

Re-measured at the current head on the same card with cases that ship in
the repository: the W7-X fixed-boundary example (`examples/data/w7x.json`,
mpol = 12, ntor = 12, nfp = 5) converges in about 3.7 s single
configuration, and `cma` runs in 0.29 s single configuration against
0.030 s per equilibrium in a distinct batch of 32, falling to 0.022 s
per equilibrium in a batch of 192 (4.2 s for all 192). The int8-scatter
and state-backup kernel measurements on this card are recorded in
`docs/cuda_acceleration.md`.

The per-kernel timing harness (`VMECPP_KERNEL_TIMING=1`) and the phase
timer give the breakdown if you want to reproduce these numbers on other
hardware.

## Runtime controls

`docs/cuda_acceleration.md` documents every environment variable. Three
classes:

1. Production-path switches, default ON, each falling back to a slower
   equivalent path (kernel fusions, the dealias and scatter warp-packs,
   pair-coarsened variants, parallel residual reduction, the three CUDA
   graphs, the device-side convergence gate, the per-configuration
   time-step controller). These exist so a
   misbehavior on untested hardware can be bisected per subsystem.
2. Opt-in throughput modes, default OFF (K-window sync elision,
   deferred-sync residuals, a whole-iteration CUDA graph under elision).
3. A mixed-precision and alternate-FFT experiments annex, default OFF,
   with measured outcomes recorded per gate (FP32/TF32 scatter variants
   with double-single compensation, Ozaki-slice multiplications on FP32
   and on int8 tensor cores with exact integer accumulation, a
   Carson-Higham iterative refinement on the RZ tridiagonal solve,
   hand-coded radix-8x3 DFTs). None of these are on the production path.
   They are included because the negative results are documented in code
   and prevent re-exploration; if you prefer the annex out of this PR, I
   will split it into a follow-up.

`VMECPP_CUDA_DEVICE` selects the device ordinal for the process
(default 0; multi-GPU batches run one process per device). The
run-scoped controls (`VMECPP_N_CONFIG_MAX`, `VMECPP_SYNC_ELIDE`,
`VMECPP_PER_CFG_NITER_CAP`, and the batch upscale gates) are re-read at
the start of every run, so successive runs in one process can carry
different settings.

## Known limitations

- The `<M>` (volume-averaged spectral width) column and the `specw`
  output evaluate the current iteration state under the CUDA build; the
  CPU path evaluates the restart backup, which lags by one iteration, so
  the printed column can differ in the trailing digit at a given
  iteration.
- In distinct mode the per-configuration `OutputQuantities` derivation
  is the default (`VMECPP_PER_CFG_RECOMPUTE=0` opts out, falling back to
  a hot-restart reconstruction per configuration); the subprocess
  recompute (`recompute_outputs_from_spectra`) and the raw converged
  spectra (`return_spectra=True`) remain available.

## Suggested review order

################################################################################
author: CharlesCNorton | opened: 2026-07-08 12:29 UTC
branch: CharlesCNorton:cuda-enzyme-jacobian -> main

+1221 -82, vmecpp/vmec/ideal_mhd_model/ideal_mhd_model.h +41 -0, vmecpp/vmec/ideal_mhd_model/jacobian_kernel.h +68 -24, vmecpp/vmec/ideal_mhd_model/nestor_async.h (added) +190 -0, vmecpp/vmec/ideal_mhd_model/phase_timer.h (added) +190 -0, vmecpp/vmec/output_quantities/output_quantities.cc +19 -3, vmecpp/vmec/output_quantities/output_quantities.h +15 -0, vmecpp/vmec/pybind11/BUILD.bazel +1 -0, vmecpp/vmec/pybind11/pybind_vmec.cc +1857 -3, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec.cc +919 -10, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec.h +57 -0, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec_test.cc +102 -0, tests/test_adjoint.py +7 -0, tests/test_autodiff.py +4 -0, tests/test_autodiff_ncurr1.py +4 -0, tests/test_cuda_gpu.py (added) +545 -0, tests/test_external_optimizers.py +6 -0, tests/test_hessian.py +6 -0, tests/test_internal_gradient.py +5 -0, tests/test_iteration.py +4 -0, tests/test_preconditioner.py +7 -0, tests/test_signgs.py +10 -0

--- description ---
**Change** - `jacobian_kernel.h` factors the per-point arithmetic of the half-grid Jacobian into `HalfGridJacobianPoint`, marked `__host__ __device__` under a CUDA compiler, and `ComputeHalfGridJacobian` calls it, so the CPU solver loop and a CUDA kernel share one formula. `cuda_enzyme_jacobian_jvp.cu` applies `__enzyme_fwddiff` to that function in a device kernel, one thread per half-grid point, over device buffers of inputs, input tangents, outputs and output tangents. `VMECPP_ENABLE_CUDA_ENZYME`, default OFF, requires `VMECPP_USE_CUDA=ON`, a Clang CUDA compiler and `VMECPP_ENZYME_PLUGIN`, and builds the standalone `cuda_enzyme_jacobian_jvp_test`, registered with CTest as `cuda_enzyme_jacobian_jvp`. Resolves #609.

**Cause** - #609 asks for one CUDA physics kernel differentiated by Enzyme on the device. Enzyme differentiates Clang's device IR, not nvcc's, so the option needs a Clang CUDA compiler. The half-grid Jacobian is a pure per-point function: differentiating it touches no cuFFT plan, CUDA graph, persistent device state or host transfer of the #561 path.

**Evidence** - the device tangents of `r12`, `ru12`, `zu12`, `rs`, `zs` and `tau` agree with the CPU-Enzyme derivative of the same function to between 2e-16 and 3e-14, and with Richardson-extrapolated central differences to below 1e-8, for a full tangent and for each input block seeded alone. Configuring with CUDA off, with a non-Clang CUDA compiler or without the plugin stops with a message naming the missing requirement.

**Tests** - `cuda_enzyme_jacobian_jvp_test` holds the device JVP to 1e-8 of the CPU-Enzyme derivative and to 1e-6 of the finite differences, first for a full tangent and then for one geometry block at a time, where an output that does not depend on the seeded block gets an exactly zero tangent. It needs a GPU and the ClangEnzyme plugin, so CI does not run it. `jacobian_kernel_autodiff_test`, the CPU Enzyme test of `ComputeHalfGridJacobian`, runs on the refactored loop.

**Scope** - the option is OFF by default, and the default CPU, CPU-Enzyme and CUDA builds run the same Jacobian arithmetic as before through `HalfGridJacobianPoint`. Stacked on #561; the diff reduces to this change once #561 merges.

PR #636 [OPEN, draft] third_party: BIEST and SCTL as fetched dependencies
################################################################################
author: jurasic-pf | opened: 2026-07-11 17:41 UTC
branch: proximafusion:07-11-vendor_biest_sctl -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/636
files (14): CMakeLists.txt +53 -0, MODULE.bazel +1 -1, MODULE.bazel.lock +136 -1, third_party/biest/BUILD.bazel (added) +22 -0, third_party/biest/README.md (added) +17 -0, third_party/biest/biest.BUILD (added) +22 -0, third_party/biest/test_biest.cc (added) +87 -0, third_party/biest/warm_start.patch (added) +28 -0, third_party/mkl/BUILD.bazel (added) +6 -0, third_party/mkl/mkl_fftw.BUILD (added) +19 -0, third_party/non_module_deps.bzl +34 -0, third_party/sctl/BUILD.bazel (added) +10 -0, third_party/sctl/cxx20.patch (added) +12 -0, third_party/sctl/sctl.BUILD (added) +28 -0

--- description ---
Adds BIEST (https://github.com/dmalhotra/BIEST) and its SCTL dependency (both Apache-2.0) as **pinned, patched, fetched dependencies** — Bazel via the `non_module_deps` extension, CMake via `FetchContent`. Only the build overlays, the local patches, and our test live in-repo, as the foundation for the BIEST free-boundary solver (next PR in the stack).

Local patches relative to upstream:
- **`warm_start.patch` (BIEST)**: warm-start the iterative solve in `ExtVacuumField::ComputeBplasma` from the previous sigma solution (SCTL's GMRES natively accepts a non-empty initial guess; upstream never passes one). Cuts the solve cost when the boundary changes little between calls — exactly the free-boundary VMEC iteration pattern.
- **`cxx20.patch` (SCTL)**: removes a constructor declared with a template-id, ill-formed under `-std=c++20`.

**FFT provider / licensing:** SCTL needs an FFT via the FFTW3 API — without one its fallback FFT materializes dense DFT matrices and OOMs at realistic resolutions (measured). FFTW itself is GPL and cannot be linked into MIT-licensed VMEC++, so the FFTW3 API is provided by **Intel MKL's FFTW interface** (Ubuntu: `libmkl-dev`; the `@mkl_fftw` repo/`MKL_ROOT` discovery points at the system installation). LAPACKE comes from the BSD netlib packages (`liblapacke-dev`). Wheel builds need both present in the build image.

Part 1/4 of the free-boundary solver stack.

related to https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/issues/628

PR #637 [OPEN, draft] free_boundary: BIEST solver, dual-run diagnostics, standalone harness
################################################################################
author: jurasic-pf | opened: 2026-07-11 17:41 UTC
branch: proximafusion:07-11-biest_free_boundary_solver -> 07-11-vendor_biest_sctl
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/637
files (22): docs/free_boundary_solvers.md (added) +114 -0, pyproject.toml +2 -0, src/vmecpp/__init__.py +7 -0, src/vmecpp/_mkl_preload.py (added) +38 -0, vmecpp/common/vmec_indata/vmec_indata.cc +27 -10, vmecpp/common/vmec_indata/vmec_indata.h +4 -0, vmecpp/free_boundary/CMakeLists.txt +2 -0, vmecpp/free_boundary/biest/BUILD.bazel (added) +16 -0, vmecpp/free_boundary/biest/CMakeLists.txt (added) +5 -0, vmecpp/free_boundary/biest/biest.cc (added) +301 -0, vmecpp/free_boundary/biest/biest.h (added) +94 -0, vmecpp/free_boundary/dual_solver/BUILD.bazel (added) +14 -0, vmecpp/free_boundary/dual_solver/CMakeLists.txt (added) +5 -0, vmecpp/free_boundary/dual_solver/dual_solver.cc (added) +96 -0, vmecpp/free_boundary/dual_solver/dual_solver.h (added) +85 -0, vmecpp/vmec/pybind11/pybind_vmec.cc +2 -0, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/BUILD.bazel +2 -0, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec.cc +109 -19, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec.h +19 -0, vmecpp_tools/free_boundary_standalone/BUILD.bazel (added) +20 -0, vmecpp_tools/free_boundary_standalone/free_boundary_standalone.cc (added) +272 -0, tools/plot_fb_dual_run.py (added) +257 -0

--- description ---
Adds **BIEST as a user-selectable free-boundary solver** (`free_boundary_method = "biest"`), plus the diagnostic tooling used to validate it.

## BIEST solver
Solves the same exterior Neumann problem as NESTOR (`B_vac · n = 0`, prescribed net toroidal current) with high-order partition-of-unity singular quadrature + GMRES, replacing NESTOR's low-order singularity subtraction + dense LU. New input `biest_accuracy_digits` (default 8, range [1,14]) plumbed through JSON/HDF5/pybind/pydantic.

Design notes:
- coil `B·n` computed with BIEST's own normals (`ComputeBdotN`) — the normal-orientation convention stays internal to BIEST; net current enters as `Jplasma = signgs·μ0·I_tor`.
- **full solve on the fresh boundary every update.** Frozen-field or frozen-operator schemes were measured to stall or slow VMEC's descent (dual-run diagnostics below); per-iteration fresh solves converge in *fewer* iterations than NESTOR (421 vs 489 on CTH-like) with ~25 % lower converged DELBSQ. Experimental partial updates remain behind `VMECPP_BIEST_PARTIAL_DRIFT_TOL`.
- thread-cooperative: partition deposit → pinned-thread solve → partition readout. The solve must run on the *same* thread every update (solver state persists) — an `omp single` is not sufficient.

## Validation (cth_like_free_bdy, mpol=5, nzeta=36, 43 kA)
| check | result |
|---|---|
| bsqvac NESTOR vs BIEST | 0.83 % rel RMS — equals NESTOR's discretization floor |
| BIEST self-convergence (digits 8 vs 12) | 2e-9 RMS |
| I_tor from solution | 43228.9 A (input 43229.08; NESTOR: 43227.1) |
| in-loop | ftol 1e-10 in 421 iterations |

## Diagnostics
- `DualSolver`: run any solver with the other as a shadow (`VMECPP_FB_SHADOW`, `VMECPP_FB_DUAL_DUMP`); one JSONL record per vacuum update with both |B|²/2 fields + boundary coefficients.
- `tools/plot_fb_dual_run.py`: side-by-side imshow (primary | shadow | diff), poloidal-spectrum evolution across the iteration, scalar traces.
- `vmecpp_tools/free_boundary_standalone`: A/B harness evaluating any solver at a fixed boundary.
- `docs/free_boundary_solvers.md`.

Known cost: BIEST runs serially inside the OpenMP parallel region (~2.8 s per solve at digits 8 on CTH) — it is the accuracy reference; use Vac2 (next PR) for fast accurate production runs.

Part 2/4, stacked on https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/636.

related to https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/issues/628

PR #638 [OPEN, draft] free_boundary: Vac2 (Strumberger) solver with stellarator-symmetry fold
################################################################################
author: jurasic-pf | opened: 2026-07-11 17:41 UTC
branch: proximafusion:07-11-vac2_free_boundary_solver -> 07-11-biest_free_boundary_solver
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/638
files (22): CMakeLists.txt +2 -1, docs/free_boundary_solvers.md +38 -4, src/vmecpp/__init__.py +3 -0, vmecpp/common/vmec_indata/vmec_indata.cc +4 -0, vmecpp/common/vmec_indata/vmec_indata.h +5 -1, vmecpp/free_boundary/CMakeLists.txt +1 -0, vmecpp/free_boundary/vac2/BUILD.bazel (added) +43 -0, vmecpp/free_boundary/vac2/CMakeLists.txt (added) +11 -0, vmecpp/free_boundary/vac2/basis_integrals.cc (added) +218 -0, vmecpp/free_boundary/vac2/basis_integrals.h (added) +51 -0, vmecpp/free_boundary/vac2/vac2.cc (added) +323 -0, vmecpp/free_boundary/vac2/vac2.h (added) +94 -0, vmecpp/free_boundary/vac2/vac2_mode_assembly.cc (added) +192 -0, vmecpp/free_boundary/vac2/vac2_mode_assembly.h (added) +78 -0, vmecpp/free_boundary/vac2/vac2_solver.cc (added) +1842 -0, vmecpp/free_boundary/vac2/vac2_solver.h (added) +150 -0, vmecpp/vmec/pybind11/pybind_vmec.cc +1 -0, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/BUILD.bazel +1 -0, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec.cc +33 -0, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec.h +11 -0, vmecpp_tools/free_boundary_standalone/BUILD.bazel +1 -0, vmecpp_tools/free_boundary_standalone/free_boundary_standalone.cc +13 -1

--- description ---
Adds **Vac2 (Strumberger/Tichmann) as a third free-boundary solver** (`free_boundary_method = "vac2"`), a C++ port of the Strumberger/Tichmann vac2 Fortran solver (formulation context: Merkel & Strumberger, arXiv:1508.04911).

Vac2 solves the same exterior Neumann problem as NESTOR with a reformulated kernel that avoids the factorial growth of NESTOR's analytic `c_mn` coefficients: accuracy improves monotonically with resolution instead of saturating at mpol ≈ 12–16.

## Why this matters
On cth_like_free_bdy at **production resolution**, Vac2's vacuum pressure agrees with BIEST (the accuracy reference) to **2.0e-3 rel RMS vs NESTOR's 8.3e-3**, and improves to 7.3e-4 at ntheta=64 while NESTOR stays pinned at its formulation floor. In-loop it converges in 420 iterations (NESTOR: 489) to the accurate DELBSQ floor (9.4e-4 vs NESTOR's 1.23e-3) — i.e. **BIEST-class accuracy at near-NESTOR cost**.

## Performance (best-of-3, CTH-like full run)
| configuration | wall |
|---|---|
| Vac2 (this PR) | 2.4 s |
| NESTOR, full updates every iteration | 0.58 s |
| NESTOR production (frozen-matrix partials) | 0.33 s |

Levers implemented:
- **stellarator-symmetry column fold** in the kernel assembly: the mirrored column's row-FFT is the complex conjugate of the canonical one, so its mode arrays are exact sign-flipped copies — verified to 5e-16 against the unfolded path (`VAC2_NO_SYMM_FOLD=1` kill switch). Halves the dominant O(nuv²) work.
- bounded nested OpenMP team for the solve inside VMEC's parallel region (otherwise it runs serial on the pinned thread); `VMECPP_VAC2_SOLVE_THREADS` override.
- NESTOR-style frozen-operator partial updates (cached Cholesky factor + fresh RHS) implemented but **opt-in** (`VMECPP_VAC2_PARTIAL_UPDATES=1`): identical converged answer but ~2.6× iterations — the same frozen-operator descent pathology observed with BIEST partials.
- the FFT path uses the FFTW3 API via Intel MKL's FFTW interface (FFTW itself is GPL).

## Wrapper conventions (following the Fortran vac2 reference)
Full poloidal grid, u,v ∈ [0,1); surface-area-weighted normal `signgs·(Xu×Xv)` (**not** normalized — normalizing produced a smooth 9 % bsqvac error with perfect current integrals); `bexn = +(B_coil·n)` with the coil field only — net current via the secular term `curtor = μ0·I_tor`; `curpol = ⟨xv·B⟩`.

Limitations: `lasym` not yet supported by the wrapper; `nzeta == 1` unverified (NESTOR covers axisymmetric); known follow-ups: stage-3 symmetry fold, FFTW plan caching.

Part 3/4, stacked on https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/637.

related to https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/issues/628


################################################################################
PR #639 [OPEN, draft] Shafranov-shift pressure scan across free-boundary solvers
################################################################################
author: jurasic-pf | opened: 2026-07-11 17:41 UTC
branch: proximafusion:07-11-shafranov_shift_cases -> 07-11-vac2_free_boundary_solver
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/639
files (1): benchmarks/shafranov_shift_scan.py (added) +231 -0

--- description ---
Adds the **Shafranov-shift pressure-scan benchmark** comparing the free-boundary solvers on identical configurations, in the spirit of the verification studies in Hudson et al., Phys. Plasmas 32, 043906 (2025) and Conlin et al., arXiv:2412.05680: at high pressure the plasma (virtual-casing) contribution to the vacuum field dominates, making the axis shift the sensitive observable for free-boundary solver quality.

Cases:
- **solovev** (axisymmetric, nfp=1): promoted to ntor=1/nzeta=36 with an in-memory coil response so the 3D-only solvers (BIEST, Vac2) can run it; pressure scaled to percent-level beta.
- **cth_like** (3D, nfp=5): pressure scan on the standard fixture.

First results:

**cth_like (3D, nfp=5, prescribed 43 kA):**
- axis shift grows to **+36 mm at ⟨β⟩ ≈ 4.9 %** before the equilibrium is lost to self-intersecting surfaces at scale 40;
- NESTOR and Vac2 agree to ~30 μm at low beta, but the discrepancy **grows to ~1.1 mm at β ≈ 2.1 %** — direct evidence that the virtual-casing contribution amplifies solver-discretization differences, which is exactly the regime this benchmark targets.

**solovev (axisymmetric run as 3D, ncurr=0 self-consistent current):**
- NESTOR: very large clean shifts, R_axis 4.10 m → **7.78 m at ⟨β⟩ = 15 %** — the demanding large-virtual-casing regime this benchmark targets;
- NESTOR-vs-Vac2 axis discrepancy: 3.9 mm at β≈0 growing to **15 mm at β = 1.3 %** (Vac2's kernel quadrature is coarse on the full-torus nfp=1 grid); Vac2 aborts above β ≈ 5 % (Cholesky loses positive-definiteness at extreme boundary deformation — graceful error propagation instead of CHECK is a noted follow-up);
- **methodology lesson baked into the script**: at percent-level beta the shifted boundary silently left the fixture's r ≤ 6 vacuum-field box and the clamped field corrupted all solvers (initially masqueraded as a 12 cm solver discrepancy). The in-memory response box is widened accordingly, and "Plasma Boundary exceeded Vacuum Grid Size" warnings should be treated as fatal for scan purposes.

**BIEST arbitration (digits 6):**
- CTH at β = 2.1 %: BIEST R_axis = 0.76328 — NESTOR +0.20 mm, Vac2 +1.29 mm (at β = 0.19 % BIEST sits ~0.4 mm below both).
- solovev at β = 1.3 % (wide box): BIEST R_axis = 5.18581 — NESTOR **+1.4 mm**, Vac2 **−14 mm**.

Consistent picture: NESTOR's converged axis tracks the BIEST reference to mm-level even at high beta, while Vac2's converged equilibria carry a larger error that grows with beta — notable because Vac2's *fixed-boundary* vacuum pressure is the more accurate one on CTH. Understanding this in-loop discrepancy (self-consistent current coupling? full-torus kernel resolution at nfp=1?) is the top follow-up for the Vac2 solver.

Bring-up notes recorded in the script:
- VMEC++ fails in the first iterations for `ntor = 0` with `nzeta > 1`; the axisymmetric case must be promoted to ntor=1 with zeroed non-axisymmetric modes.
- `makegrid_parameters_solovev.json` requests raw (current-scaled) fields; the in-memory path must use `normalize_by_currents = true`.
- the solovev_free_bdy fixture aborts with VAC-VMEC I_TOR MISMATCH at 32 OpenMP threads (**pre-existing on main**, unmodified fixture) — the scan pins `max_threads = 4`. Probably worth a separate issue.

Part 4/4, stacked on https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/638.

tests/test_signgs.py +10 -0

--- description ---
**Change** - `VMECPP_USE_HIP`, default OFF, Linux only and mutually exclusive with `VMECPP_USE_CUDA`, compiles the GPU translation units of #561 as HIP and links hipFFT. `CMAKE_HIP_ARCHITECTURES` defaults to `gfx90a;gfx942`, and `VMECPP_HWCAPS_DISPATCH` defaults off as under CUDA. The new `hip_compat.cuh` maps the CUDA runtime, stream, event, graph and cuFFT calls of those units onto HIP one to one. The masked `__shfl_*_sync` intrinsics map to the width-32 legacy forms, which partition the 64-lane wavefront into 32-lane groups and keep the 32-lane warp semantics the butterfly reductions assume; `__syncwarp` maps to a wave-level barrier. `__launch_bounds__` keeps its first parameter only, since the second is min blocks per multiprocessor in CUDA and min waves per execution unit in HIP. The five NVIDIA-only scatter experiments (the `nvcuda::wmma` and cuBLAS paths, opt-in and default OFF) are compiled out under HIP, and setting one of their variables falls back to the production scatter with a one-time notice. `docs/cuda_acceleration.md` gains a HIP section.

**Cause** - the GPU iteration body of #561 builds for NVIDIA GPUs only. The HIP build defines `VMECPP_USE_CUDA`, which the host dispatch sites test to mean the GPU iteration body is built, together with `VMECPP_USE_HIP`, which selects the backend inside the GPU units, so the host solver code is the same for both backends, and every production-path switch, the batched mode, the graphs and the diagnostics apply unchanged.

**Evidence** - ROCm 7.14 with hipcc, targeting gfx90a, at 341ad6ad: all six GPU translation units compile, four emit device code (`fft_toroidal_cuda_kernels.cu` a 513 KB fatbin with 115 kernels) and the other two are host-dispatch units with empty fatbins; the CMake path configures and builds the core library with `-DVMECPP_USE_HIP=ON`; enabling both backends stops at configure, and HIP without ROCm stops at `enable_language(HIP)`. The build has not run on an AMD GPU, and the HIP link step as it now sits inside `vmecpp_add_core` has not been built with ROCm. With `VMECPP_USE_HIP` undefined the GPU units and their headers preprocess to the same lines as on the #561 branch. On an RTX 3070 Ti at 341ad6ad, `tests/test_cuda_gpu.py` passes with both `VMECPP_TEST_CUDA_SLOW` W7-X cases, and the `cma` wout volume and aspect are bit-identical to the #561 branch by hex comparison, as is the CPU-only build.

**Tests** - none added; `tests/test_cuda_gpu.py` of #561 is the GPU regression suite, and it needs a CUDA build and a GPU.

**Scope** - the option is OFF by default; the delta is six files and the new header. Stacked on #561; the diff reduces to this change once #561 merges.

PR #755 [OPEN] Run the multi-grid reference tests for every ns_array entry
################################################################################
author: CharlesCNorton | opened: 2026-09-01 00:51 UTC
branch: CharlesCNorton:profiles-multi-ns -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/755
files (4): vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec.cc +20 -9, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec.h +10 -2, vmecpp_large_cpp_tests/vmec/radial_profiles/radial_profiles_test.cc +131 -108, vmecpp_large_cpp_tests/vmec/vmec/vmec_test.cc +82 -53

--- description ---
**Change** - `CheckRadialProfiles` and `CheckMultigridResult` run every entry of `ns_array`, not only the first. `Vmec::run` gains `checkpoint_multi_grid_step`, default 1, which selects the multi-grid step the `SPECTRAL_CONSTRAINT`, `RADIAL_PROFILES_EVAL` and `SETUP_INITIAL_STATE` checkpoints of `InitializeRadial` fire in. `INTERP` stays ungated, since it is only reached when interpolating from a coarser grid. The `CheckRadialProfiles` reference filenames take a retry index of 0 in place of `get_num_eqsolve_retries()`.

**Cause** - educational_VMEC dumps `profil1d`, `profil3d` and `multigrid_result` for every entry of `ns_array`, but the checkpoints in `InitializeRadial` fired in whichever step came first, which left the setup of every later step unreachable, and both tests carried a TODO limiting them to the first step. `get_num_eqsolve_retries()` is cumulative across steps, while the trailing index of the dumps counts retries within a step, which at a setup checkpoint is zero.

**Evidence** - worst deviation of `CheckMultigridResult` from the reference per step, and the bound the test holds it to:

| case | steps (ns) | worst | bound |
|---|---|---|---|
| solovev | 5, 11, 55 | 1.4e-15, 1.2e-13, 5.5e-12 | 5e-15, 1e-12, 5e-11 |
| solovev_no_axis | 5, 11 | 3.2e-15, 2.2e-14 | 5e-15, 2e-13 |
| cma | 25, 51 | 7.5e-13, 1.4e-11 | 1e-11, 1e-10 |

The deviation grows with each step, since a later step starts from the interpolated solution of the previous one. In `CheckRadialProfiles` only solovev needs a bound per step: `DBL_EPSILON / 2`, 5e-16 and 5e-16.

**Tests** - the two tests themselves. Their `DataSource` gains `multigrid_tolerances`, one entry per `ns_array` entry; left empty, every step uses `tolerance`.

**Scope** - `checkpoint_multi_grid_step` defaults to 1 and `is_checkpoint_step` to true, so existing callers checkpoint in the first step as before. No solver arithmetic changes.


################################################################################
PR #765 [OPEN] Add proof-carrying equilibrium certificates via Stellarocq
################################################################################
author: CharlesCNorton | opened: 2026-09-01 16:15 UTC
branch: CharlesCNorton:proof-carrying-equilibria -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/765
files (3): docs/index.md +1 -0, docs/proof_carrying_equilibria.md (added) +91 -0, examples/make_equilibrium_certificate.py (added) +126 -0

--- description ---
**Change** - `docs/proof_carrying_equilibria.md`, linked from the docs index, describes three uses of [Stellarocq](https://github.com/CharlesCNorton/stellarocq) with VMEC++: certificates of the force balance of a wout, theorems and enclosures about the reconstructed field, and an oracle for the solver. `examples/make_equilibrium_certificate.py` runs VMEC++ on an input file, saves the wout, and calls the generator, the checker and the wout guard of a Stellarocq checkout; `--checker` takes the statically linked checker from the Stellarocq releases, `--mpol`, `--ntor`, `--ns` and `--ftol` override the resolution and the force tolerance of the input, `--project` asks the checker for the harmonics of the residual at the modes of the run, and without `--stellarocq` the example saves the wout and stops, which is how the example job of CI runs it. A certificate lists, for a set of full-grid nodes and angles, per-component bounds on the mu0-scaled residual `J x B - grad p` of the field reconstructed from the wout Fourier coefficients by VMEC's half-grid rule; every numeric input is written exactly, as a dyadic rational. With `--cells` the bounds are claimed over cells that tile the poloidal angle instead of at sampled angles.

**Cause** - "this wout satisfies force balance" rests on VMEC++ and on its agreement with Fortran VMEC. The checker is extracted from the Rocq development in Stellarocq and shares no code with either solver or with the generator: it recomputes the residual from the coefficients in verified interval arithmetic (CoqInterval over the Flocq formalization of IEEE-754). A VALID verdict is therefore a theorem about those exact inputs, and a division by zero, an invalid square root or an unlucky rounding in the evaluation gives INVALID. What remains trusted is the Rocq kernel with the classical real axioms and the primitive float and integer specifications of its standard library, extraction and the OCaml compiler, and a parsing driver; `make audit` in Stellarocq prints the axioms behind every theorem. Certifying a stellarator-symmetric equilibrium through the non-stellarator-symmetric reconstruction found #788, fixed in #789.

**Evidence** - Stellarocq at `main`, 20 worker processes. The example, with vmecpp 0.7.4, ran `examples/data/solovev.json` as a point and as a cell certificate, `examples/data/input.cth_like_fixed_bdy` and `test_data/up_down_asym.json`, each to a VALID verdict with every input slot of the certificate equal to the wout's, with the checker built in the checkout and with the released static one. Point certificates, six nodes per case; the bound is the largest of the three component bounds, over the reference `B^2` scale the generator prints:

| case | points | largest bound, of the field scale | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| `wout_solovev` (axisymmetric, ns=55, 6 modes, power series) | 48 | 7.3e-5 | VALID, under 0.1 s |
| `examples/data/w7x.json` as shipped (3D, nfp=5, 288 modes, ns=99) | 192 | 1.3e-2 | VALID, 3.7 s |
| `wout_cth_like_fixed_bdy` (3D, nfp=5, 41 modes, ns=25, two-power pressure, `PRES_SCALE` 432.29) | 192 | 1.0e-2 | VALID, 0.2 s |
| `up_down_asym.json` (non-stellarator-symmetric, ns=17) | 48 | 1.2e-2 | VALID, under 0.1 s |
| `wout_cma` (3D, nfp=2, 59 modes, ns=51) | 192 | 5.2e-2 | VALID, 0.3 s |
| solovev with one `rmnc` coefficient of a certified stencil perturbed by 0.1% | 48 | same claim | INVALID, under 0.1 s |

Every stellarator-symmetric case also certifies through the non-stellarator-symmetric reconstruction with its antisymmetric coefficients set to zero, at the same bounds (`gen/make_cert.py --force-lasym`).

For a three-dimensional equilibrium the bound follows the resolution and the force tolerance of the run. `input.li383_low_res`, which ships with 25 modes, 16 surfaces and a tolerance of 1e-6, run by the example with `--ns 31 --ftol 1e-14` and the Fourier resolution raised:

| `--mpol`, `--ntor` | modes | largest bound, of the field scale |
|---|---|---|
| 4, 3 | 25 | 3.0e-1 |
| 6, 4 | 50 | 4.1e-2 |
| 8, 6 | 98 | 1.0e-2 |
| 10, 8 | 162 | 6.8e-3 |
| 12, 10 | 242 | 5.6e-3 |

With the input's own tolerance the last row is 3.1e-2. Past about two hundred modes the bound stays near 6e-3 at this radial resolution.

A spectral solution balances forces mode by mode over the modes it retains, and what it leaves pointwise is the truncation. `--project` follows a point certificate with the largest mean harmonic of each component over the modes of the run and the angles of a node, a finite sum that `harm_encloses` of Stellarocq encloses. Over 48 by 48 angles per node, of the field scale:

| case | largest pointwise bound | `r_s` harmonic | `r_u` harmonic | `r_v` harmonic |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `input.li383_low_res` at `--mpol 12 --ntor 10 --ns 31 --ftol 1e-14` | 6.7e-3 | 9.0e-4 | 9.7e-5 | 6.1e-5 |
| `examples/data/w7x.json` as shipped | 1.9e-2 | 2.1e-3 | 3.9e-5 | 7.1e-5 |

Cell certificates:

| case | cells | worst cell bound | of the field scale | tighten | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `wout_solovev` (axisymmetric, ns=55) | 49152 | 1.3e-5 | 1.9e-4 | 30 s | VALID, 41 s |
| `wout_circular_tokamak_reference` (axisymmetric, ns=17) | 49152 | 1.5e-2 | 2.5e-4 | 33 s | VALID, 48 s |
| `wout_cma` (3D, nfp=2, 59 modes, ns=51) | 24576 | 1.3e-2 | 4.3e-2 | 50 s | VALID, 90 s |
| `wout_li383_low_res_reference` (3D, nfp=3, 25 modes, ns=16) | 24576 | 8.7e-1 | 2.4e-1 | 24 s | VALID, 39 s |
| `input.li383_low_res` at `--mpol 12 --ntor 10 --ns 31 --ftol 1e-14` (242 modes) | 24576 | 2.7e-2 | 7.4e-3 | 254 s | VALID, 628 s |

The two axisymmetric cases carry 6 nodes of 8192 poloidal cells covering the whole angular torus. An axisymmetric equilibrium has every `n` zero, so its toroidal derivative encloses to zero and one cell spans the whole toroidal angle. A three-dimensional one has to resolve the toroidal direction as finely as the poloidal, which squares the cell count, so the three-dimensional cases carry 3 nodes of 4096 poloidal cells at each of 2 toroidal angles. `check_ccert_correct`, the theorem behind a cell verdict, walks from the centre of a cell to any point of it by a mean-value step in each angle, with the derivative enclosed over the whole cell.

The page also records, from the same checkout: the `(2,1)` harmonic of `r_s` on the `iota = 1/2` surface of the circular tokamak under a `(1,1)` ripple, 2.1e-6, 2.2e-6 and 1.8e-6 at 65, 129 and 257 surfaces while every other harmonic falls fourfold per doubling, and the same harmonic falling fourfold on a profile without the rational surface; the certified Mercier current term at `s = 0.125` of `wout_up_down_asym`, -3.7e-3 against the file's -6.1e-4 at sixteen surfaces, both falling to zero by 257; the two-term quasisymmetry ratio of `wout_nfp4_QH_ns50`, whose certified harmonic ratios span 1.7 where an axisymmetric case spans 2e-4; four invariances of VMEC++ holding to rounding; the Boozer harmonics of `|B|` at `s = 0.417` of `wout_solovev` enclosing `booz_xform`'s coefficients; and a discrete equilibrium of the reconstruction, exactly one zero of the collocated residual on one to three surfaces of `wout_solovev`, in a box shaped to the coefficients, its radii from 5.7e-10 to 6.3e-9, around a centre Newton's method moved 2.2e-6 to 3.5e-6 from the wout's coefficients.

**Tests** - none; certification is offline and per wout. The example job of CI runs the script without arguments, which runs VMEC++ on `solovev.json` and saves the wout.

**Scope** - an example script and a documentation page. The example imports `vmecpp` and calls a Stellarocq checkout through `subprocess`; the solver, its dependencies and CI are untouched, and the proof development, the generator and the checker stay in Stellarocq.


################################################################################
PR #769 [OPEN] Add opt-in Anderson acceleration of the descent iteration
################################################################################
author: CharlesCNorton | opened: 2026-09-01 17:53 UTC
branch: CharlesCNorton:anderson-acceleration -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/769
files (13): src/vmecpp/__init__.py +9 -0, vmecpp/common/vmec_indata/vmec_indata.cc +20 -0, vmecpp/common/vmec_indata/vmec_indata.h +5 -0, vmecpp/common/vmec_indata/vmec_indata_test.cc +4 -0, vmecpp/vmec/pybind11/pybind_vmec.cc +2 -0, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/BUILD.bazel +8 -2, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/CMakeLists.txt +2 -0, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/anderson_acceleration.cc (added) +119 -0, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/anderson_acceleration.h (added) +70 -0, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec.cc +158 -0, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec.h +42 -0, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec_test.cc +54 -0, tests/test_iteration.py +23 -0

--- description ---
**Change** - `VmecInput.anderson_acceleration`, off by default, switches on Anderson acceleration of the descent iteration (JSON and HDF5 round trip through `VmecINDATA`); `Vmec::SetAndersonAcceleration(window, start_iteration, frequency, zero_velocity_on_jump)` keeps the tuning parameters for C++ callers. One Garabedian time step maps the Fourier state x to g(x). `AndersonAcceleration` keeps a short per-thread history of map outputs and residuals r = g - x; the team solves the least-squares problem for the combination of recent residual differences that best cancels the current residual, each thread applies that combination to its map outputs, and the momentum restarts. A thread stores the history of its own slice over its full stored radial range, satellite points included, so the combination stays consistent at the partition seams. The k x k normal equations are the only cross-thread data: `SumInFixedTree` reduces them over a binary tree fixed by thread id, they are Tikhonov-regularized by 1e-12 of their mean diagonal, and a combination that is not finite or exceeds 100 in magnitude is skipped. The defaults are a window of 2, engagement 25 iterations after a stage starts or restarts, and disengagement once fsqr + fsqz + fsql falls below max(1e-4, 1e-3 x the residual at stage entry, 1e3 ftol). Free-boundary runs are included.

**Cause** - the extrapolation pays in the nonlinear phase at the start of each multigrid stage, where the descent is still finding the basin. In the tail the fixed-point map is linear and the momentum iteration already converges at its optimal rate; extrapolating there held the residual in a limit cycle on the QUASR fixed-boundary cases at ftol 1e-9, so the tail is handed back to the descent. An earlier study ran Anderson from fsq = 1e-6 down, where it loses.

**Evidence** - iterations to convergence on the QUASR configurations of `tests/test_free_boundary_quasr.py` (ns [8, 16, 31], mpol = ntor = 6, ftol 1e-9, 8 threads), summed over the multigrid stages. Fixed boundary:

| case | plain | accelerated |
|---|---|---|
| 954 | 414 | 394 |
| 9914 | 739 | 546 |
| 19493 | 2186 | 1816 |
| 19609 | 1719 | 1472 |
| 19940 | 1311 | 1122 |
| 29346 | 1079 | 959 |
| 50136 | 3866 | 3894 |
| 65579 | 3342 | 3279 |
| 112718 | 1789 | 1671 |
| 165868 | 1288 | 1152 |
| 167381 | 1277 | 1197 |
| 336902 | 1314 | 1280 |
| total | 20324 | 18782 |

Free boundary:

| case | plain | accelerated |
|---|---|---|
| 954 | 1512 | 1414 |
| 9914 | 1339 | 1021 |
| 19609 | JACOBIAN_75_TIMES_BAD | 1433 |
| 19940 | 1546 | 1285 |
| 29346 | 1391 | 1302 |
| 65579 | 3212 | 2672 |

The other six free-boundary cases do not converge either way. Accelerated and plain runs converge to the same equilibrium: over the twelve fixed-boundary cases they differ by less, in `wb`, axis, iota profile and every Fourier array, than the plain run at ftol 1e-9 differs from the plain run at ftol 1e-11. Repeated 8-thread accelerated runs agree in all 438 output datasets.

**Tests** - `AndersonAcceleratedCthMatchesPlainIteration` in `vmec_test` solves `cth_like_fixed_bdy` both ways, requires fewer iterations with the flag, and holds each equilibrium-defining quantity to at least five times its measured deviation between the two paths. `test_run_honors_anderson_acceleration_flag` in `tests/test_iteration.py` drives the input flag through `vmecpp.run`.

**Scope** - with the flag unset the window is 0, no history is allocated and the time step is the one that was there.

--- comment by CharlesCNorton, 2026-09-01 22:24 UTC ---
The environment variable is gone. The feature is the boolean `anderson_acceleration` on `VmecInput` and `VmecINDATA`, off by default.

The difference from Finding 13 is the regime. That study ran Anderson on the tail, from fsq = 1e-6 down, where it loses; the gain here is in the nonlinear phase at the start of each multigrid stage. The earlier gate mixed the two (its floor max(1e3 ftol, 1e-8) is 1e-6 on QUASR at ftol 1e-9), which is why the first QUASR runs lost. The gate is now: window 2, disengage below max(1e-4, 1e-3 x the residual at stage entry, 1e3 ftol), free boundary included.

--- comment by CharlesCNorton, 2026-09-01 23:37 UTC ---
Residual traces and surface animations, all from the PR branch with the flag off and on (8 threads, the QUASR inputs of the test suite).

Invariant residual per iteration, fixed boundary (plain blue, accelerated red; the spikes are the multigrid interpolations):

![QUASR fixed boundary residuals](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharlesCNorton/vmecpp/anderson-figures/fsq_fixed.png)

Free boundary. The cases that fail do so in the sawtooth plateau of the free-boundary descent (restart, decay to 1e-5, restart); 19609 leaves it once accelerated, and 112718 never engages because its plateau sits below the gate:

![954 surfaces](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharlesCNorton/vmecpp/anderson-figures/surfaces_quasr0000954_fixed_v2.gif)

PR #772 [OPEN] Carry the sign of the Jacobian in chi
################################################################################
author: CharlesCNorton | opened: 2026-09-02 12:49 UTC
branch: CharlesCNorton:fix/chi-jacobian-sign -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/772
files (5): vmecpp/vmec/output_quantities/output_quantities.cc +4 -1, vmecpp/vmec/output_quantities/output_quantities_test.cc +3 -1, vmecpp_large_cpp_tests/vmec/output_quantities/output_quantities_test.cc +3 -1, tests/cpp/vmecpp/vmec/pybind11/test_pybind_vmec.py +2 -1, tests/test_init.py +6 -0

--- description ---
**Change** - `chi` carries the sign of the Jacobian: `ComputeIntermediateThreed1FirstTableQuantities` forms it as `2 pi * sign_of_jacobian * chi1`, and `wout.chi` follows.

**Cause** - `chi` was accumulated as `2 pi * chi1` with `chi1 = sum(phipH * iotaH) * deltaS`, while `phi`, `phipf` and `chipf` all carry `sign_of_jacobian`. With the usual negative Jacobian the wout therefore held `chi` = minus the integral of iota over phi. Fortran VMEC has the same omission (`eqfor.f`, `chi = twopi*chi1`) and PARVMEC 10.0 reproduces it, so the reference wout files hold the negated profile.

**Evidence** - on `cth_like_fixed_bdy`, `phi(ns)` is -0.035, the integral of `chipf` over s is -0.040, and `chi(ns)` was +0.040. `iota = dchi/dphi` read back from the file had the sign opposite to `iotaf`, and the threed1 `psi`, which does carry the sign, disagreed with `chi`.

**Tests** - the five comparisons against the reference files negate the reference for `chi`, with a note at each: `CheckWOutFileContents` in `output_quantities_test`, `CheckThreed1FirstTable` in the large `output_quantities_test`, `test_output_quantities` in `test_pybind_vmec.py`, and `test_vmecwout_io` and `test_against_reference_wout` in `tests/test_init.py`.

**Scope** - nothing else reads `chi1`, and the force balance never reads `chi`, so the converged equilibrium is unchanged.

--- comment by CharlesCNorton, 2026-09-02 13:18 UTC ---
fixing

--- comment by CharlesCNorton, 2026-09-07 17:00 UTC ---
Since #584 landed, `MakeGeometry` accumulates the poloidal flux with `sign_of_jacobian`, so main now carries two poloidal fluxes of opposite sign: across solovev, cth_like_fixed_bdy, cth_like_fixed_bdy_asym, li383_low_res, cma, up_down_asym and circular_tokamak, `geometry.poloidal_flux / wout.chi` is exactly -1.000000000000.

Taking `d(chi)/d(phi)` from the file picks the same side: it matches `+iotas` to 3e-15 for the geometry profile and returns `-iotas` for `wout.chi`, which also equals minus the integral of the `chipf` stored beside it.


################################################################################
PR #784 [OPEN] Measure the discretization by the method of manufactured solutions
################################################################################
author: CharlesCNorton | opened: 2026-09-02 19:42 UTC
branch: CharlesCNorton:manufactured-solution -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/784
files (15): examples/manufactured_solution.py (added) +1710 -0, pyproject.toml +3 -0, src/vmecpp/__init__.py +7 -0, vmecpp/common/vmec_indata/vmec_indata.cc +15 -0, vmecpp/common/vmec_indata/vmec_indata.h +5 -0, vmecpp/vmec/fourier_forces/fourier_forces.cc +34 -0, vmecpp/vmec/fourier_forces/fourier_forces.h +7 -0, vmecpp/vmec/ideal_mhd_model/ideal_mhd_model.cc +44 -0, vmecpp/vmec/ideal_mhd_model/ideal_mhd_model.h +24 -0, vmecpp/vmec/pybind11/pybind_vmec.cc +60 -0, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/BUILD.bazel +14 -0, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/force_source_test.cc (added) +145 -0, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec.cc +40 -0, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec.h +14 -0, tests/test_manufactured_solution.py (added) +287 -0

--- description ---
**Change** - `IdealMhdModel::SetForceSource` adds a fixed spectral force to the decomposed force before the m = 1 gauge rotation, so it passes through the same chain as the force it augments. `Vmec::SetForceSource` refuses a source unless the input sets the new `enable_force_source`, keeps it, and reinstalls it after every `InitializeRadial`, which an axis reguess and each multi-grid step trigger; a source installed for another radial resolution is dropped with a warning. `FourierForces::ActiveSpans` lists the parities a run carries in one fixed order, which is the layout of the source. `VmecModel.set_force_source` exposes it to Python, and `VmecModel.set_state_from_fourier` sets the state from combined-basis coefficients through `FourierGeometry::InitFromState`, the routine a hot restart already uses, so the basis conventions keep one implementation. `examples/manufactured_solution.py` runs the studies below.

**Cause** - the reference tests compare against Fortran VMEC, which cannot say whether either code converges to the continuum problem. With the source set to the negative of the continuum ideal-MHD force of an analytic mapping, that mapping is the exact solution of the modified problem, and the distance from the solver to the mapping is discretization error.

**Evidence** - helical axis, rotating ellipse, non-trivial lambda; `nfp = 3`, aspect ratio 18, `beta(0) = 2` per cent, `mpol = 5`, `ntor = 3`, 24 x 24. Errors are relative L2 over `s >= 0.1`.

| ns | energy | R, Z force | lambda force | solved R, Z | solved lambda |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 25 | 2.858e-08 | 2.590e-04 | 1.715e-05 | 7.980e-05 | 1.755e-03 |
| 51 | 6.586e-09 | 5.866e-05 | 3.999e-06 | 1.116e-05 | 1.810e-04 |
| 101 | 1.646e-09 | 1.426e-05 | 9.586e-07 | 2.463e-06 | 7.014e-05 |
| 201 | 4.116e-10 | 3.525e-06 | 2.347e-07 | 4.593e-07 | 1.614e-05 |
| order | 2.00 | 2.02 | 2.03 | 2.42 | 2.12 |

Angular error falls by 9.4 for every two grid points, from 3.2e-06 at 16 x 16 to 4.2e-11 at 26 x 26. The source drops the force at the mapping by 5.4e+04 at `ns = 25` and 7.9e+06 at `ns = 201`, and the solve reaches `fsq = 1.5e-16` at every resolution.

The m = 0 lambda is the exception. Its force is second order, 8.5e-09 at `ns = 201`, while its state error sits at 1e-02 and does not fall, so this is conditioning and not consistency. `FourierGeometry::extrapolateTowardsAxis` copies that component from the first interior surface onto the axis, and the force transform does not transpose the copy.

Asymmetric and constrained current: the mapping gains a sine series in R and a cosine series in Z and lambda at m <= 1, obeying `RSC_{1n} = ZCC_{1n}`, and measures 2.01 for the R, Z force and 2.04 for lambda, with the source cancelling by 4.8e+07 at `ns = 201`. The symmetric mapping run through the asymmetric path reproduces the symmetric energy error exactly at every resolution. With `ncurr = 1` the solver solves `chi'` from `currH(s) = <B_u>(s)` instead of reading an iota profile; prescribing the mapping's own `<B_u>` as a `cubic_spline_i` profile returns its iota and leaves the order at 2.01.

Free boundary: `only_coils` takes the vacuum field from the mgrid with no Laplace solve, and a field bilinear in (R, Z) is interpolated exactly, so the vacuum pressure is analytic. At the last radial node only the half-grid cell inside the boundary exists, so the force there is `(1/ds) dw/dx_s + (1/2) EL(w) + edge`, whose first and third terms are the pressure jump `(1/ds) R (p_total - p_vac) (z_u, -r_u)`.

| ns | volume | last node | fsq with source |
|---|---|---|---|
| 25 | 4.034e-05 | 1.837e-05 | 3.463e-11 |
| 51 | 9.319e-06 | 4.232e-06 | 4.794e-12 |
| 101 | 2.277e-06 | 1.058e-06 | 1.542e-12 |
| 201 | 5.758e-07 | 2.645e-07 | 1.134e-12 |
| order | 2.00 | 2.00 | |

The edge condition is second order, like the volume. There is no free-boundary counterpart to the solved-state columns: the free-boundary descent limit-cycles around `fsq = 1e-09` for 40000 iterations, where the fixed-boundary one reaches 1.5e-16, so the state it stops at is not a solution of the modified problem.

**Tests** - `tests/test_manufactured_solution.py` pins the second order of the symmetric, asymmetric, constrained-current and free-boundary forces, the geometric angular truncation, the cancellation of the force at the mapping, the rejections (a source of the wrong length, a source without `enable_force_source`) and the survival of a source across a reinitialization, in 8 seconds. `SlicingIsIndependentOfThreadCount` in `//vmecpp/vmec/vmec:force_source_test` holds the source's effect on the assembled force to within 1e-12 of the source itself across 1, 2, 4 and 8 radial threads, which a source landing on the wrong surfaces would miss by its own size.

**Scope** - without `enable_force_source` a source is refused and a run is unchanged. `sympy` becomes a test extra and the test module skips without it; `scipy` is needed only by `--study fit`.

PR #795 [OPEN] Carry the Jacobian sign in the Mercier volume derivative
################################################################################
author: CharlesCNorton | opened: 2026-09-04 19:55 UTC
branch: CharlesCNorton:mercier-jacobian-sign -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/795
files (2): vmecpp/vmec/output_quantities/output_quantities.cc +4 -1, vmecpp_large_cpp_tests/vmec/output_quantities/output_quantities_test.cc +1 -1

--- description ---
**Change** - `ComputeMercierStability` multiplies the volume derivative of the first Mercier table by `sign_of_jacobian`, as it already does for WELL at the same site.

**Cause** - the volume derivative was the plain half-grid average of `vp_real`. `mercier.f90:191` forms it as

```fortran
sqs = p5*(vp_real(i) + vp_real(i+1))*sign_jac
```

and then writes `shear(i)/sqs` as SHEAR, `sqs` itself as VP, `ip(i)/sqs` as ITOR' and `presp(i)/sqs` as PRES'. The Jacobian sign is therefore carried by all four columns, and with the usual negative Jacobian, which is every bundled case, they came out negated.

**Evidence** - ratio to the `mercier.<case>` file educational_VMEC writes, before the change, to the five significant figures the file carries, on every case:

| column | ratio before |
| --- | --- |
| SHEAR | -0.999987 |
| VP | -1.000005 |
| ITOR' | -0.999999 |
| PRES' | -0.999996 |
| WELL | 0.999978 |
| IOTA | 0.999974 |

S, PHI, PRES and ITOR agree as well. Comparing every column of both Mercier tables against that file across the bundled cases, nine go from four differing columns to all fifteen agreeing: `solovev`, `solovev_no_axis`, `solovev_analytical`, `cth_like_fixed_bdy`, `cth_like_fixed_bdy_iota`, `cth_like_fixed_bdy_nzeta_37`, `cth_like_fixed_bdy_spline_pressure`, `cth_like_free_bdy` and `li383_low_res`. What still differs elsewhere does not come from this quantity: `cma` carries no current, so its ITOR and ITOR' are round-off around 1e-10; the asymmetric cases differ by the `tmult` normalisation; and `up_down_asymmetric_tokamak` sits in a different poloidal gauge.

**Tests** - `CheckMercierStability` reproduced the same unsigned average and asserted the first table against it, so the test and the implementation agreed with each other and both differed from the reference. The test now applies `sign_of_jacobian` where it already reads it for the WELL comparison.

**Scope** - the second table is unaffected, since `Dshear`, `Dcurr`, `Dwell` and `Dgeod` are built from the intermediates and not from this quantity. Nothing outside the output stage reads these fields.

--- comment by CharlesCNorton, 2026-09-06 19:57 UTC ---
Checked independently against the `mercier.<case>` text file educational_VMEC writes, rather than the dumps: on main the SHEAR, VP, ITOR' and PRES' columns come out with the opposite sign for `cth_like_fixed_bdy` and `solovev`, and on this branch all ten columns agree to the five digits the file prints.

PR #799 [OPEN] Recover cold initial-Jacobian failures through the coarse bootstrap
################################################################################
author: philippbogdan | opened: 2026-09-06 10:08 UTC
branch: philippbogdan:fix/initial-jacobian-bootstrap -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/799
files (18): .github/workflows/test_conda_env.yaml +4 -3, benchmarks/initial_jacobian_recovery/README.md (added) +127 -0, benchmarks/initial_jacobian_recovery/cases.csv (added) +54 -0, benchmarks/initial_jacobian_recovery/control-results.csv (added) +61 -0, benchmarks/initial_jacobian_recovery/controls.csv (added) +6 -0, benchmarks/initial_jacobian_recovery/environment.json (added) +60 -0, benchmarks/initial_jacobian_recovery/recovery-results.csv (added) +2083 -0, benchmarks/initial_jacobian_recovery/run.py (added) +306 -0, .gitattributes +1 -0, vmecpp/test_data/BUILD.bazel +6 -0, vmecpp/test_data/initial_jacobian_recovery.json (added) +805 -0, vmecpp/test_data/initial_jacobian_recovery.md (added) +16 -0, vmecpp/test_data/wout_initial_jacobian_recovery.nc (added) +3 -0, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/BUILD.bazel +1 -0, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec.cc +29 -5, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec.h +5 -1, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec_test.cc +72 -0, tests/test_initial_jacobian_recovery.py (added) +34 -0

--- description ---
A cold run can reject its first radial grid with a bad Jacobian even though a three-surface bootstrap reaches the requested equilibrium. Both the inner solver and grid driver returned before the existing fallback could execute. Allow that initial failure to reach the driver, restore the input axis and radial state, and retry once at three surfaces before the original grid sequence. Correct the logger's stage index for the inserted stage. The public single-grid solver keeps its existing interface and error handling.

On a frozen set of 53 historical QUASR initial-Jacobian failures, the baseline converges on 0 cases and this change on the same 15 cases in each of three repetitions: 5 of 12 discovery cases and 10 of 41 held-out cases. Both use identical boundary coefficients, profiles, field tables, final resolution and `1e-9` tolerance. This is an initialization-robustness result on a selected stress set, not a population failure-rate or physical-accuracy claim.

The PR includes the frozen source hashes, portable paired benchmark, measured ledgers, and regression tests. The numerical tests compare the complete recovered geometry and force history with an explicit cold-three solve. A regenerated educational_VMEC reference checks geometry, lambda, iota, pressure and flux. The corresponding Fortran fix is [educational_VMEC #29](https://github.com/jonathanschilling/educational_VMEC/pull/29).

Validation details and physical-diagnostic limits are recorded in `benchmarks/initial_jacobian_recovery/README.md`. Native VMEC tests pass on macOS arm64; the paired Python regression run found the same six failures on baseline and the recovery implementation, with 201 passes, two skips and 36 deselections. That comparison preceded the final interface-preserving wrapper refactor; native tests, Python reference checks and independent API probes were repeated on the final build. Independent API checks cover fixed/free boundaries, finite pressure/current, one/four threads, all output modes, hot starts, later-stage failures and insufficient budgets.
