PR #814 [OPEN, draft] Propose shipping Enzyme-enabled kernels in pip wheels (follow-up to #710)
################################################################################
author: Copilot | opened: 2026-09-06 21:23 UTC
branch: proximafusion:copilot/suggest-pip-wheels-shipping -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/814
files (3): .github/workflows/build_enzyme_wheels.yaml (added) +228 -0, docs/enzyme_wheel_packaging.md (added) +165 -0, docs/index.md +1 -0

--- description ---
#710 wires an Enzyme-derived exact JVP/VJP of the force kernel into `vmecpp_core` behind `-DVMECPP_ENABLE_ENZYME=ON`, but that flag is never set in the `cibuildwheel`-built pip wheels — it's only exercised by a dedicated CI job with its own from-scratch Clang/LLVM + Enzyme toolchain. This adds a design proposal for closing that gap.

## Changes

- **`docs/enzyme_wheel_packaging.md`**: proposal doc covering
  - Reusing the existing CMake "prebuilt Enzyme object" mode (`VMECPP_ENZYME_JVP_OBJECT`/`VMECPP_ENZYME_VJP_OBJECT`) instead of installing Clang/LLVM/Enzyme inside `manylinux`/macOS `cibuildwheel` containers
  - Compiling the two Enzyme translation units in a small, separate, cacheable CI job per wheel target, then feeding the resulting `.o` files into the normal wheel build via `CMAKE_ARGS`
  - Why this keeps every other translation unit compiled exactly as today, so non-adjoint users see no change to the code they exercise, and why Enzyme introduces no new runtime dependency (it only runs at build time)
  - Concrete checks for no performance regression: symbol/disassembly diffing of unaffected translation units, and extending the existing exact-HVP benchmark with a forward-solve timing comparison run against an actual built wheel
  - Open questions: which wheel targets to prioritize, prebuilt-object staleness/versioning, Windows being out of scope for now
- **`docs/index.md`**: links the new doc from the toctree

## Scope

This is a design proposal only, not an implementation. The actual toolchain and CI wiring is substantial, cross-platform work intended to be done and validated incrementally in follow-up PRs, building on this proposal.

--- comment by Copilot, 2026-09-07 08:30 UTC ---

################################################################################
PR #816 [OPEN] Reject a profile that its evaluator cannot evaluate
################################################################################
author: CharlesCNorton | opened: 2026-09-07 05:34 UTC
branch: CharlesCNorton:fix/spline-minimum-knots -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/816
files (4): vmecpp/common/vmec_indata/vmec_indata.cc +53 -14, vmecpp/common/vmec_indata/vmec_indata_test.cc +109 -0, vmecpp/vmec/profile_parameterization_data/profile_parameterization_data.cc +58 -26, vmecpp/vmec/profile_parameterization_data/profile_parameterization_data.h +9 -2

--- description ---
**Change** - `IsConsistent` rejects a profile its evaluator cannot evaluate. The parameterization table records, per name, the knots a spline evaluator needs (4 for the Akima and cubic families, 2 for the line segments) and the coefficients a closed form needs (`gauss_trunc` 2, `two_power` and `two_power_gs` 3, `two_lorentz` 8; the zero-padded power series accept any count), and `CheckProfile` rejects an input that carries fewer. Spline knots must increase strictly, and the Akima and cubic families, which are evaluated only inside their knots, must run from 0 to 1; the line segments continue their end segments and may stop short. `NeedsSplineData()` becomes the knot count being positive, so its one caller is unchanged.

**Cause** - `CheckProfile` required a spline profile to carry knots and the two arrays to have the same length, but not to be long enough to evaluate. Below their counts the evaluators return `0.0` for every `s` after one warning: `evalAkima` and `evalCubic` need four knots, `evalLineSegment` two, and `gauss_trunc`, `two_power`, `two_power_gs` and `two_lorentz` need 2, 3, 3 and 8 coefficients. The Akima and cubic evaluators also return `0.0` outside their knots. That is the failure the validation added in #719 exists to prevent, in its own words: an unusable parameterization "reaches the solver as a zero profile, which converges to a silently wrong equilibrium." Fortran VMEC halts instead. `profile_functions.f` counts the knots and stops before the evaluator: `IF (i < 4) STOP 'pcurr: check s-grid for curr-grid values!'`, with the same guard on the pressure and iota branches.

**Evidence** - `cth_like_fixed_bdy.json` unchanged, and then with `pmass_type = "cubic_spline"` over three and four knots, on main:

| | `two_power` (as shipped) | `cubic_spline`, 3 knots | `cubic_spline`, 4 knots |
|---|---|---|---|
| exit | converged | converged | converged |
| `presf(0)` | 4.322928e+02 | 0.000000e+00 | 4.322908e+02 |
| `betatotal` | 1.926384e-03 | 0.000000e+00 | 1.681736e-03 |
| iterations | 121 | 119 | 121 |

The three-knot run writes a `wout` reporting a converged zero-pressure equilibrium while the input asked for 432 Pa. With `ncurr = 1` the same holds for the prescribed current, as in #798.

**Tests** - in `vmec_indata_test`: `CheckSplineProfilesNeedEnoughKnots` drives the three spline families one knot short of and at their minimum for the pressure profile and holds the current and iota profiles to the same counts; `CheckClosedFormProfilesNeedEnoughCoefficients` does the same for the four closed forms; `CheckSplineKnotsIncreaseAndSpanTheRadius` rejects unordered and repeated knots and Akima or cubic knots that stop short of 0 or 1, and accepts line segments that do.

**Scope** - validation only; the evaluators are untouched. An input rejected now is one whose profile evaluated to zero over part or all of the radius.

--- comment by CharlesCNorton, 2026-09-08 14:55 UTC ---
Extended to the other ways a profile reaches the solver as zero. The closed-form parameterizations have the same gap as the splines: below their coefficient count `gauss_trunc`, `two_power`, `two_power_gs` and `two_lorentz` log a warning and return zero for every s, so `pmass_type = two_power` with `am = [1000]` converges to a zero-pressure equilibrium; the table now carries the count each needs (2, 3, 3, 8) beside the knot count. The spline evaluators walk their knots in order and the Akima and cubic ones return zero outside them, where Fortran VMEC stops with `iflag = -2` and `-1`; knots out of order gave a different equilibrium without a message (beta 1.80e-3 against 1.68e-3 for the same five points on `cth_like_fixed_bdy`), and knots ending at 0.9 gave zero pressure beyond them. `IsConsistent` now requires strictly increasing knots for every spline and knots from 0 to 1 for the Akima and cubic families; the line segments continue their end segments and may stop short. `CheckClosedFormProfilesNeedEnoughCoefficients` and `CheckSplineKnotsIncreaseAndSpanTheRadius` cover it.

--- review by jurasic-pf, 2026-09-25 21:46 UTC: APPROVED ---


################################################################################
PR #820 [OPEN] Add an opt-in undamped lambda preconditioner
################################################################################
author: CharlesCNorton | opened: 2026-09-07 12:14 UTC
branch: CharlesCNorton:lambda-preconditioner-undamped -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/820
files (9): src/vmecpp/__init__.py +3 -0, vmecpp/common/vmec_indata/vmec_indata.cc +21 -0, vmecpp/common/vmec_indata/vmec_indata.h +4 -0, vmecpp/vmec/ideal_mhd_model/ideal_mhd_model.cc +9 -3, vmecpp/vmec/ideal_mhd_model/ideal_mhd_model.h +2 -1, vmecpp/vmec/pybind11/pybind_vmec.cc +2 -0, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec.cc +2 -1, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec_test.cc +76 -0, vmecpp/vmec/vmec_constants/vmec_algorithm_constants.h +7 -0

--- description ---
**Change** - `VmecInput.undamped_lambda_preconditioner`, off by default (JSON and HDF5 round trip through `VmecINDATA`). Set, the lambda preconditioner scales its assembled diagonal by `1 / lamscale^2` alone, through `kLambdaPreconditionerUndampedFactor`; unset, the scale stays `kLambdaPreconditionerDampingFactor / (4 lamscale^2)`.

**Cause** - the `1 / lamscale^2` converts the internally rescaled lambda coefficients. The remaining factor of 0.5 comes from VMEC, where `lamcal.f90` sets `damping_fac = 2.0` beside a commented-out `pfactor0/4` marked "sometimes helps convergence"; neither the Fortran nor the VMEC++ comment gives a derivation for it.

**Evidence** - iterations summed over the multigrid schedule, single-threaded, each case at its own ftol. The twelve QUASR configurations of `tests/test_free_boundary_quasr.py`, run in fixed-boundary mode at mpol = ntor = 6 and ns_array = [8, 16, 31]:

| case | off | on |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 954 | 141 | 133 |
| 9914 | 328 | 261 |
| 19493 | 819 | 689 |
| 19609 | 587 | 447 |
| 19940 | 492 | 416 |
| 29346 | 431 | 432 |
| 50136 | 1325 | 1331 |
| 65579 | 905 | 772 |
| 112718 | 636 | 509 |
| 165868 | 441 | 377 |
| 167381 | 464 | 386 |
| 336902 | 461 | 367 |
| total | 7030 | 6120 |

and the fixed-boundary cases of the test suite:

| case | off | on |
| --- | --- | --- |
| solovev | 204 | 217 |
| solovev_analytical | 400 | 397 |
| circular_tokamak | 749 | 589 |
| cma | 207 | 148 |
| cth_like_fixed_bdy | 121 | 113 |
| cth_like_fixed_bdy_iota | 118 | 111 |
| li383_low_res | 124 | 133 |
| up_down_asym | 594 | 635 |
| cth_like_fixed_bdy_asym | 137 | 132 |
| total | 2654 | 2475 |

That is 13 percent fewer iterations over the QUASR set and 7 percent over the test suite, at no cost per iteration, since the change is the value of a scalar computed once per preconditioner update. The gain is not uniform. Three of the nine test-suite cases take more iterations, and on the W7-X example the count is not a smooth function of the factor: scanning 2.0 through 6.0 gives 2954, 2699, 3604, 3616, 3520, 2580, 2609 and 3017, a twenty percent swing in either direction with no trend.

The converged state moves with the descent path. At a case's own ftol the two settings stop about 1e-3 apart in `iotaf`; held to 1e-12 they are 4.1e-6 apart on `cth_like_fixed_bdy` and 5.1e-6 on `cth_like_fixed_bdy_asym`, and the enclosed volume is identical at every tolerance. What remains at 1e-12 is the spectral-condensation angle gauge, which moves the poloidal spectrum without moving the flux surfaces: `zmns` differs by 2.9e-4 where `wb` agrees to 2.3e-8. Changing only the thread count leaves all of these below 3e-14.

**Tests** - in `vmec_test`, on `solovev`, `cth_like_fixed_bdy` and `cth_like_fixed_bdy_asym`: `UndampedLambdaPreconditionerLeavesTheFirstForceResidual` requires `fsqt(0)` to be bit-identical between the two settings, since the scale multiplies an already assembled force, and both runs to reach the tolerance; `UndampedLambdaPreconditionerConvergesToTheSameEquilibrium` compares `rmnc`, `zmns`, `iotaf` and `wb` at ftol 1e-12 against five times the measured deviation.

**Scope** - unset, the assembled preconditioner is bit-identical to the current one, which every case above reproduces exactly.

PR #823 [OPEN, draft] feat(solver): Residual-Aware Dynamic Preconditioning (RAD-P) for Issue #818
################################################################################
author: duushgu | opened: 2026-09-07 16:38 UTC
branch: duushgu:feature/adaptive-preconditioner-update -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/823
files (12): src/vmecpp/__init__.py +4 -0, vmecpp/common/flow_control/flow_control.cc +1 -0, vmecpp/common/flow_control/flow_control.h +3 -0, vmecpp/common/vmec_indata/vmec_indata.cc +21 -0, vmecpp/common/vmec_indata/vmec_indata.h +4 -0, vmecpp/common/vmec_indata/vmec_indata_test.cc +5 -0, vmecpp/vmec/ideal_mhd_model/ideal_mhd_model.cc +94 -11, vmecpp/vmec/ideal_mhd_model/ideal_mhd_model.h +7 -4, vmecpp/vmec/pybind11/pybind_vmec.cc +2 -0, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec.cc +8 -1, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec_test.cc +27 -0, tests/test_iteration.py +30 -0

--- description ---
**Change**

Replaces the fixed 25-iteration preconditioner update interval with a residual-aware decision function (`shouldUpdateRadialPreconditioner`) controlled by a new `VmecInput` field `adaptive_preconditioner_update` (default `false`).

**Cause**

`kPreconditionerUpdateInterval = 25` is a compile-time constant in `ideal_mhd_model.cc`. The value is inherited from the original Fortran VMEC without derivation. In early multigrid stages the residual drops orders of magnitude per dozen iterations; a 25-step-old block-tridiagonal factor can misalign the metric and trigger `BAD_JACOBIAN` resets. In late stages with sub-percent per-step improvement the full refactor is wasted cost.

**Evidence**

Tested across 21 configurations: 9 standard test suite equilibria (including `up_down_asym`) and 12 QUASR stellarator configurations.

| Suite | Baseline iters | RAD-P iters | Delta |
|:---|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| Standard (9 cases) | 5,775 | 5,681 | -94 (-1.6%) |
| QUASR (12 cases) | 20,451 | 19,272 | -1,179 (-5.8%) |
| **Total** | **26,226** | **24,953** | **-1,273 (-4.85%)** |

Notable: `quasr_65579` -915 iterations (-27.2%), `up_down_asym` -105 iterations (-3.3%).

`max |Delta_R| < 4e-4` across all 21 configurations relative to baseline with flag off.

**Tests**

- `vmec_test.cc`: `AdaptivePreconditionerMatchesEquilibrium` -- runs 4 equilibria with flag on and off; asserts `max |Delta_R| < 1e-3`. Tolerance set to the numerical noise expected from trajectory differences when the converged state is identical.
- `tests/test_iteration.py`: `test_adaptive_preconditioner_matches_equilibrium` -- same 4 configurations at the Python level; same tolerance.

**Scope**

`adaptive_preconditioner_update` defaults to `false`. Execution is bit-identical to current code when `false`. No configuration in the 21-case benchmark regresses in iteration count.

PR #832 [OPEN] Implement the sum_cossq current profiles
################################################################################
author: CharlesCNorton | opened: 2026-09-08 14:38 UTC
branch: CharlesCNorton:implement-sum-cossq-current-profiles -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/832
files (5): vmecpp/common/vmec_indata/vmec_indata.cc +36 -0, vmecpp/common/vmec_indata/vmec_indata_test.cc +32 -0, vmecpp/vmec/radial_profiles/radial_profiles.cc +92 -0, vmecpp/vmec/radial_profiles/radial_profiles.h +3 -0, vmecpp/vmec/radial_profiles/radial_profiles_test.cc +105 -0

--- description ---
**Change** - `pcurr_type = sum_cossq_s`, `sum_cossq_sqrts` and `sum_cossq_s_free` now evaluate the enclosed current of their cos^2 humps in closed form, as `pcurr` in Fortran VMEC does. `IsConsistent` requires `ac[0]` of the two equidistant forms to be an integer hump count from 2 to 20, and a positive half-width for every hump of the free form that carries an amplitude.

**Cause** - the three names sat in the parameterization table as allowed current profiles, but `evalProfileFunction` fell through to its default branch, which returns 0.0 after one line on stderr. With `ncurr = 1` the run continued with zero current everywhere and reported `ctor = 0`; `VmecInput` types `pcurr_type` as a plain string and `IsConsistent` only checks that the name is in the table, so nothing stopped it.

**Evidence** - `cth_like_fixed_bdy` with each of the three profiles, VMEC++ against PARVMEC 10.0 reading the same INDATA file at `ftol = 1e-11`: identical iteration counts (565, 513 and 620) and every wout field within 1e-6, `rmnc` within 1.2e-8, `iotaf` within 3e-8 and `jcurv` within 1e-13. On main the three inputs converge to one and the same currentless equilibrium in 744 iterations, `ctor` = 1e-11 A and an edge iota of 0.29 against the 43 kA and 0.83 to 0.89 they prescribe.

**Tests** - `SumCossqSIntegratesItsHumps`, `SumCossqSqrtsIntegratesItsHumpsInRho` and `SumCossqSFreeIntegratesItsHumps` in `radial_profiles_test` compare the closed forms with a quadrature of the hump densities and the end humps cut by s = 0 and s = 1; `CheckSumCossqProfilesNeedAValidHumpLayout` in `vmec_indata_test` covers the rejections.

**Scope** - the other parameterizations are untouched, and no reference file changes since educational_VMEC does not implement these profiles. At s = 0 exactly the enclosed current is 0, where the Fortran branch returns half the first hump; the solver evaluates the profile on the half grid and at s = 1 only.

PR #833 [OPEN] Let the vacuum potential's Fourier cutoffs exceed the plasma's
################################################################################
author: CharlesCNorton | opened: 2026-09-08 15:55 UTC
branch: CharlesCNorton:vacuum-potential-cutoffs -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/833
files (14): src/vmecpp/__init__.py +14 -0, vmecpp/common/sizes/sizes.cc +24 -2, vmecpp/common/vmec_indata/vmec_indata.cc +54 -0, vmecpp/common/vmec_indata/vmec_indata.h +9 -0, vmecpp/common/vmec_indata/vmec_indata_test.cc +39 -0, vmecpp/vmec/handover_storage/handover_storage.cc +15 -8, vmecpp/vmec/handover_storage/handover_storage.h +9 -0, vmecpp/vmec/ideal_mhd_model/ideal_mhd_model.cc +3 -2, vmecpp/vmec/output_quantities/output_quantities.cc +2 -2, vmecpp/vmec/pybind11/pybind_vmec.cc +3 -1, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec.cc +19 -5, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec.h +2 -0, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec_in_memory_mgrid_test.cc +68 -0, tests/test_free_boundary.py +25 -0

--- description ---
**Change** - `VmecInput.vacuum_mpol` and `VmecInput.vacuum_ntor` set the Fourier cutoffs of NESTOR's scalar potential independently of the plasma's `mpol` and `ntor`, which they default to. The potential and the vacuum field keep the plasma's tangential grid; the boundary is zero-padded into the larger layout, `nzeta` has to carry the toroidal cutoff, and `potvac` is written at the potential's own size.

**Cause** - Fortran VMEC expands the vacuum potential to `mf = mpol + 1`, `nf = ntor`, so the vacuum solution is truncated at the plasma's resolution. On the cth boundary that truncation, not the singular quadrature, is the floor #628 discusses: an exact exterior Neumann problem (a line of dipoles inside the surface, whose total field on it is zero) gives a residual of 5.3e-3 relative to the external field with `ntor = 4` for the potential at any `mpol` up to 32 and any grid up to `ntheta = 160`, `nzeta = 288`, and 4.8e-4 with the potential at `ntor = 8`. Against BIEST at twelve digits on the real cth vacuum field, NESTOR's rms error at `mpol = 16` is 1.42e-3 with `ntor = 4`, 3.5e-4 at 6, 9.9e-5 at 8 and 7.3e-5 at 12.

**Evidence** - in the loop on `cth_like_free_bdy` at `ftol = 1e-11` the change moves the boundary by tens of micrometres at its 0.19 percent beta and by 0.27 mm (`mpol = 5`) to 1.3 mm (`mpol = 8`) in `rmax_surf` at 2.1 percent beta on the wide mgrid; the axis moves by up to 76 micrometres. The numbers above were measured with the cross-term sign of #825; with the historical sign the vacuum error stays at 6.9e-3 for every cutoff, so the two changes only pay together.

**Tests** - `VacuumCutoffsStayAboveThePlasmaResolution` in `vmec_indata_test` (defaults, rejection below the plasma's cutoffs and of an `nzeta` that cannot carry `vacuum_ntor`, JSON round trip), `VacuumPotentialCutoffsAboveThePlasmaResolution` in `vmec_in_memory_mgrid_test` (the expanded run converges on the same grid, reports `potvac` at its size and stays within the vacuum resolution change of the base run), `test_vacuum_potential_cutoffs` in `tests/test_free_boundary.py`.

**Scope** - defaults are bit-identical to main: the vacuum `Sizes` equals the plasma's and the hand-off layout is unchanged. Raising the cutoffs costs only the vacuum solve, which grows with the potential's mode count.

PR #834 [OPEN] Reject an external field without stellarator symmetry when lasym is false
################################################################################
author: CharlesCNorton | opened: 2026-09-08 17:17 UTC
branch: CharlesCNorton:reject-asymmetric-external-field -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/834
files (3): vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec.cc +90 -0, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec_in_memory_mgrid_test.cc +62 -0, tests/test_free_boundary.py +38 -0

--- description ---
**Change** - a free-boundary run with `lasym = false` now rejects an external field that is not stellarator symmetric. `Vmec::run` samples the field on the initial boundary and on its mirror image and returns `kInvalidArgument` when the rms antisymmetric part exceeds 1e-4 of the field, naming the measured value; `lasym = true` is unaffected.

**Cause** - with `lasym = false` the vacuum solver samples the external field on the poloidal half range and the transforms extend it by stellarator symmetry, so a field with an antisymmetric part is replaced by a different field without any message, as in Fortran VMEC. The equilibrium then differs from the one the field produces: the cth coils raised by 0.2 mm, whose only physical effect is a rigid 0.2 mm shift of the plasma, move `rmax_surf` by 0.7 mm and the edge iota by 6e-4 relative to the `lasym = true` result; at 1 mm the boundary is off by 3.6 mm, at 5 mm by 26 mm and the edge iota by 2 percent. On an exact exterior Neumann problem (an interior current loop 6 cm off the axis) the symmetric path reports the enclosed current with a 14 percent error.

**Evidence** - the antisymmetric part on the cth boundary is 3.2e-4 of the field for the 0.2 mm shift, 1.6e-3 for 1 mm and 7.9e-3 for 5 mm, and the boundary error of the `lasym = false` run is about twice that fraction of `rmax_surf`; the shipped cth field measures 2e-14 and the Solovev free-boundary field 6e-8. At the limit of 1e-4 the coils are off by 0.06 mm and the unnoticed boundary error would be 0.2 mm. Every free-boundary case in the test suites passes the check.

**Tests** - `ExternalFieldWithoutStellaratorSymmetryNeedsLasym` in `vmec_in_memory_mgrid_test` (the raised coils rejected with `lasym = false`, accepted with `lasym = true`) and `test_external_field_without_stellarator_symmetry_needs_lasym` in `tests/test_free_boundary.py`.

**Scope** - the check runs once per `run`, costs two interpolations of the boundary, and only fires for `lasym = false` free-boundary runs whose field fails the symmetry by more than the limit; a boundary outside the vacuum grid is still reported by the vacuum solver.

PR #839 [OPEN] Hand every force iteration to a callback and watch a solve converge
################################################################################
author: CharlesCNorton | opened: 2026-09-09 00:05 UTC
branch: CharlesCNorton:live-solve-view -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/839
files (15): README.md +7 -0, docs/examples_overview.md +1 -0, examples/watch_solve.py (added) +530 -0, src/vmecpp/__init__.py +26 -0, src/vmecpp/_continuation.py +3 -1, vmecpp/common/util/util.cc +3 -0, vmecpp/common/util/util.h +3 -0, vmecpp/vmec/output_quantities/output_quantities.cc +105 -59, vmecpp/vmec/output_quantities/output_quantities.h +10 -0, vmecpp/vmec/pybind11/pybind_vmec.cc +66 -7, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/BUILD.bazel +4 -0, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec.cc +73 -11, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec.h +58 -4, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec_test.cc +75 -0, tests/test_iteration_callback.py (added) +230 -0

--- description ---
**Change** - `vmecpp.run` takes an `iteration_callback`, called once per force iteration with an `IterationSnapshot`: the iteration and multigrid stage, `ns`, the invariant residuals and `ftol`, the time step, the restart reason, the Jacobian resets, whether the vacuum pressure is in the force balance, the MHD energy, and the geometry of the state. The C++ loop calls it from the master thread between two barriers, so it covers every run of `vmecpp.run`, free boundary included. Returning `False` stops the run, which returns the state reached with `ier_flag = 2`, VMEC 8.52's `more_iter_flag`, added as `VmecStatus::MORE_ITERATIONS_NEEDED`. An exception raised in the callback propagates.

`examples/watch_solve.py` uses the callback to draw the flux surfaces above the force residuals while the solve runs, in a window or into a `.gif` or video file. Closing the window stops the solve.

**Cause** - the state of an iteration was reachable only through the Python-driven loop of `vmecpp.solve_equilibrium`, which is single-threaded and does not run free-boundary cases.

**Evidence** - recorded with the example:

![Solov'ev](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharlesCNorton/vmecpp/watch-assets/watch_solovev.gif)

![CTH-like, free boundary](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharlesCNorton/vmecpp/watch-assets/watch_cth_like_free_bdy.gif)

![W7-X](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/CharlesCNorton/vmecpp/watch-assets/watch_w7x.gif)

**Tests** - `IterationCallbackSeesEveryIterationAndCanStop` in `vmec_test`: one snapshot per recorded residual, bit-identical, one converged snapshot per stage whose geometry equals the output's, and a stop at iteration 20 with `ier_flag` 2. `tests/test_iteration_callback.py`: the same through Python on solovev and the symmetric and asymmetric CTH cases, a propagating exception, the drawn cross-sections against `vmecpp.geometry.evaluate` to 1e-12, the frames of a recorded solve, and a stop when the window closes.

**Scope** - without a callback nothing changes: no barrier is added and the outputs are the ones the existing tests compare. The viewer is an example, so matplotlib stays out of the package's dependencies.

################################################################################
PR #843 [OPEN] Add a geometry tolerance that holds a run until the flux surfaces stop moving
################################################################################
author: CharlesCNorton | opened: 2026-09-09 13:47 UTC
branch: CharlesCNorton:state-convergence-measure -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/843
files (11): src/vmecpp/__init__.py +10 -0, vmecpp/common/flow_control/flow_control.h +6 -0, vmecpp/common/vmec_indata/vmec_indata.cc +22 -0, vmecpp/common/vmec_indata/vmec_indata.h +7 -0, vmecpp/vmec/handover_storage/handover_storage.cc +6 -0, vmecpp/vmec/handover_storage/handover_storage.h +6 -0, vmecpp/vmec/pybind11/pybind_vmec.cc +1 -0, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/AGENTS.md +6 -0, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec.cc +60 -2, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec.h +10 -0, tests/test_geometry_tolerance.py (added) +115 -0

--- description ---
**Change** - `VmecInput` gains `geometry_tolerance`, a distance in metres, default 0 which leaves everything as it is. When positive, a multigrid step converges only once the force residuals meet `ftol_array` AND the flux surfaces have stopped moving: `fc_.geometry_change`, the Euclidean norm of the change in the R and Z spectral coefficients over the last `nstep` iterations. `Vmec::AccumulateGeometryChange` folds each thread's contribution over the surfaces it owns, so the measure does not depend on the thread count, and it resets at every multigrid stage because the radial grid changes between stages.

**Cause** - the convergence test reads the force residual, which is small in exactly the directions the equilibrium is still moving along, so a run that meets its tolerance can stop with the quantities it is used for still in motion. On `examples/data/w7x.json` at ns = 99, a run stopped at ftol 1e-8 leaves the magnetic axis 14.7 mm from where it settles and the mid-radius rotational transform off by 3.1e-3.

**Evidence** - `examples/data/w7x.json` at ns = 99, single grid, deviation measured against the same case run to ftol 1e-16, which takes 5884 iterations:

| ftol | geometry_tolerance | iterations | axis | iota at mid-radius |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1e-8 | unset | 333 | 14.6976 mm | 3.1e-3 |
| 1e-8 | 1e-5 | 5602 | 0.0001 mm | 3.0e-7 |
| 1e-8 | 1e-6 | 7002 | 0.0003 mm | 4.0e-7 |
| 1e-12 | unset | 2954 | 0.0021 mm | 4.5e-5 |

and `cth_like_fixed_bdy` at ns = 51, against its own ftol 1e-16 run at 1963 iterations:

| ftol | geometry_tolerance | iterations | axis | iota at mid-radius |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1e-8 | unset | 245 | 0.3222 mm | 1.2e-3 |
| 1e-8 | 1e-5 | 1002 | below a micron | 4.8e-7 |
| 1e-8 | 1e-6 | 1602 | below a micron | 9.4e-8 |
| 1e-8 | 1e-7 | 2402 | below a micron | 3.5e-8 |

Ten microns of surface motion is a stricter statement about the equilibrium than ftol 1e-12 is: on W7-X it reaches iota to 3.0e-7 where ftol 1e-12 alone reaches 4.5e-5.

**Tests** - `tests/test_geometry_tolerance.py`: that the default leaves the iteration count and the wout bit-identical, that a run held to 1e-5 lands on the axis and iota of the fully converged case where the residual alone does not, that a tighter tolerance lands closer, that a negative value is rejected, and that the field survives a JSON round trip. `bazel test --config=opt //vmecpp/...` is 24 of 24 and `pytest tests/` is 234 passed, 4 skipped. Pre-commit clean.

**Scope** - unset, the convergence test is the one that was there and every existing test reproduces its iteration count. The measure is computed at the printout interval only, from a copy of the R and Z coefficients, so it costs one comparison per `nstep` iterations. The criterion can only extend a run, never shorten one. The HDF5 read is guarded on the dataset being present, so inputs written before this still load.

################################################################################
PR #845 [OPEN] Abandon a multigrid sequence whose step ends far from its tolerance
################################################################################
author: CharlesCNorton | opened: 2026-09-09 17:00 UTC
branch: CharlesCNorton:give-up-on-poor-convergence -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/845

--- description ---
**Change** - `VmecInput` gains `lgiveup` and `fgiveup`, off by default with `fgiveup` at 30, the values LIBSTELL sets. With `lgiveup` set, a multigrid step that exhausts its iteration budget with `fsqr`, `fsqz` or `fsql` still above `fgiveup` times its `ftol` ends the sequence instead of interpolating that state onto the next grid, and the run reports the step it stopped at. `README.md` drops the entry listing the logic as missing.

**Cause** - `Vmec::run` carried `TODO(jons): insert lgiveup/fgiveup logic here` at the point `runvmec.f:382` makes the same test. Without it a sequence whose coarse grid cannot reach its tolerance spends the rest of its budget on the finer grids, where every iteration costs more and the state each one starts from is the one that already failed.

**Evidence** - the twelve QUASR configurations of `tests/test_free_boundary_quasr.py`, run fixed-boundary at mpol = ntor = 6 on `ns_array` [8, 16, 31], single-threaded, over three iteration budgets:

| ftol | niter per step | stopped early | wall time, unset | set |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1e-12 | 1500 | 1 of 12 | 36.3 s | 32.9 s |
| 1e-11 | 400 | 10 of 12 | 10.6 s | 2.9 s |
| 1e-11 | 250 | 11 of 12 | 7.2 s | 1.4 s |

A configuration that stops at the first step costs 13 percent of what it costs otherwise, the two finer grids carrying the rest. The configurations that run the whole sequence take the same iterations either way: at the first budget the eleven that do not stop early converge in 1131, 1041, 1499, 1500, 1143, 1121, 1497, 1498, 1387, 1236 and 1152 iterations with the flag set and unset alike.

**Tests** - `tests/test_giveup.py`: the defaults; a three-step schedule too short to converge stopping at `ns = 5` with the flag and at `ns = 25` without it; an `fgiveup` above the residual carrying the same schedule to its last step; a converging run reproducing its iteration count and its `rmnc` and `zmns` exactly; the rejection of a non-positive `fgiveup`; and a JSON round trip. `pytest tests/` is 236 passed, 4 skipped and `bazel test --config=opt //vmecpp/...` is 24 of 24. Pre-commit clean.

**Scope** - unset, the multigrid loop is the one that was there and every existing test reproduces its iteration count. The check reads the residuals already in `FlowControl` and runs once per multigrid step. The HDF5 read is guarded on the dataset being present, so inputs written before this still load.

PR #848 [OPEN] Say what the four underived iteration constants are
################################################################################
author: CharlesCNorton | opened: 2026-09-09 21:03 UTC
branch: CharlesCNorton:iteration-constants -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/848
files (1): vmecpp/vmec/ideal_mhd_model/ideal_mhd_model.cc +9 -4

--- description ---
**Change** - `ideal_mhd_model.cc` says what `r1scale`, the free-boundary edge-force gate, the axis row of the preconditioned force norm and the growth of the constraint-force multiplier in `ns` are.

**Cause** - four markers asked what the constants are.

**Evidence** - `r1scale` is derivable. `fixaray.f90` sets `r0scale = mscale(0)*nscale(0)`, which is 1, and `residue.f90` forms `r1 = 1/(2*r0scale)**2`; with `mscale[m > 0] = nscale[n > 0] = sqrt(2)` the product is 2 for a mode with both indices non-zero, so 0.25 is the reciprocal of its square.

The edge-force gate needs `iter2 - iter1 < 50` and `fsqr + fsqz < 1e-6` together, and on a cold run the two never hold at once. Forcing the window shut so the term can never fire leaves `cth_like_free_bdy` at 489 iterations, `solovev_free_bdy` at 630 and `cth_like_free_bdy_multigrid` at 320, which is what they take as shipped, and making the tolerance always true leaves the same three numbers. Removing the window instead takes them to 598, 790 and 393.

The axis row of `fnorm1`, measured against the sum over all surfaces at the first three preconditioner updates, contributes 2.9 percent on `cma`, 1.7 on `near_axis_iota_nfp4`, 0.7 on `li383_low_res`, 0.4 on `w7x`, 0.2 on `cth_like_fixed_bdy` and nothing on `solovev` or `up_down_asym`, whose `ntor = 0` leaves no axis coefficient outside the offset already excluded. Dropping it leaves all twelve bundled fixed-boundary cases at the same iteration count, axis position and MHD energy: `fsqr1` and `fsqz1` reach the iteration only through ratios of successive residuals, which a near-constant factor cancels out of.

The growth in `ns` of the constraint-force multiplier, iterations at each case's own `ftol`, single-threaded, with the parabola and with it divided out:

| case | ns | parabola | flat |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `solovev` | 55 | 203 | 264 |
| `solovev_analytical` | 31 | 399 | 447 |
| `solovev_no_axis` | 11 | 133 | 130 |
| `circular_tokamak` | 17 | 748 | 742 |
| `cma` | 51 | 206 | 170 |
| `cth_like_fixed_bdy` | 25 | 120 | 117 |
| `cth_like_fixed_bdy_iota` | 25 | 117 | 116 |
| `li383_low_res` | 16 | 123 | 122 |
| `up_down_asym` | 17 | 593 | 593 |
| `cth_like_fixed_bdy_asym` | 25 | 136 | 130 |
| `near_axis_iota_nfp4` | 51 | 2038 | 2475 |
| `w7x` | 99 | 2953 | 1739 |
| total | | 7769 | 7045 |

and a scan of constant multipliers over the four cases that move:

| case | parabola | 1 | 1.5 | 2 | 3 | 4 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `solovev` | 203 | 264 | 228 | 205 | 189 | 203 |
| `cma` | 206 | 170 | 207 | 191 | 186 | 186 |
| `near_axis_iota_nfp4` | 2038 | 2475 | 2303 | 2150 | 2057 | 1874 |
| `w7x` | 2953 | 1739 | 1918 | 2487 | 2857 | 3620 |

`near_axis_iota_nfp4` improves monotonically out to 4 while `w7x` worsens monotonically over the same range, and every case converges to the same equilibrium either way.

**Tests** - `bazel test --config=opt //vmecpp/...` is 24 of 24 and `//vmecpp_large_cpp_tests/...` is 20 of 20. Pre-commit clean.

**Scope** - comments only.

PR #849 [OPEN] Pin the m=1 gauge in VmecModel so the equilibrium and its Jacobian are functions of the boundary
################################################################################
author: jurasic-pf | opened: 2026-09-12 15:25 UTC
branch: proximafusion:jurasic/m1-gauge-determinism -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/849
files (8): src/vmecpp/autodiff.py +67 -2, vmecpp/vmec/fourier_geometry/fourier_geometry.cc +23 -0, vmecpp/vmec/fourier_geometry/fourier_geometry.h +7 -0, vmecpp/vmec/pybind11/pybind_vmec.cc +33 -10, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec.cc +13 -1, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec.h +7 -0, tests/test_autodiff.py +5 -0, tests/test_m1_gauge.py (added) +260 -0

--- description ---
# Pin the m=1 gauge in `VmecModel` so the equilibrium and its Jacobian are functions of the boundary

## Scope

`FourierCoeffs::m1Constraint` (`fourier_coefficients.cc`) stores, for every `m = 1, n >= 1` mode, the pair `(rss + zcs)/2` and `(rss - zcs)/2`; the second one, in the `zcs` slot, is a poloidal-angle origin per toroidal harmonic (`rss = zcs` is the quasi-polar constraint). `FourierForces::zeroZForceForM1` zeroes its force, but `IdealMhdModel::update` only does so when `always_fix_m1_gauge || fsqz < 1e-6 || iter2 < 2` (`ideal_mhd_model.cc`, around line 941). The native `solve()` therefore lets the gauge drift under its force during the first iterations and freezes it wherever it is once `fsqz < 1e-6`: the converged state is a history-dependent function of the boundary.

Measured on `examples/data/cth_like_fixed_bdy.json` with `ncurr = 0` and the wout's iota profile prescribed (`ai = [1.239, 0.328, -0.768]`), `ns = 25`, `ftol = 1e-16`:

- the gauge moves by 8.5e-5 during the native solve, against a gauge size of 7.4e-5;
- a direct `ns = 25` solve and a `13 -> 25` multigrid solve of the same boundary differ by 5e-6 in `r_cc`, 4e-5 in `z_cs` and 1.2e-3 in `lambda_cs`;
- `ExactHessianVectorProduct(Transpose)` in `pybind_vmec.cc` hard-wired `fix_m1_gauge = true` although the C++ kernels take the flag, so the Jacobian behind `vmecpp.autodiff` was the fixed-gauge one and did not describe the native solve.

This PR makes the fixed-gauge solve available on `VmecModel`, gives `evaluate` and the exact Hessian-vector products one gauge semantics, and lets `vmecpp.autodiff` solve and differentiate the pinned-gauge system. The native `run()` and the default `VmecModel.solve()` are unchanged.

## Tests and measurements

New `tests/test_m1_gauge.py` (cth_like, `ncurr = 0`, `ns = 25`, `ftol = 1e-16`, Enzyme-dependent tests skip without the exact Jacobian):

- `test_pinned_gauge_stays_at_the_boundary_interpolation`: the converged gauge entries equal the initial guess to 1e-12 (measured: 0.0) and equal `sqrt(s)` times the boundary gauge to 1e-15; the native solve moves them by 8.5e-5.
- `test_pinned_gauge_solve_is_independent_of_the_multigrid_history`: direct `ns 25` vs `13 -> 25`, pinned: gauge entries identical (0.0), `r_cc, r_ss, z_sc, z_cs` agree to 2.2e-8, 1.4e-8, 1.6e-8, 1.4e-8, `lambda_sc, lambda_cs` to 3.4e-7, 1.4e-6 (the convergence floor at `ftol = 1e-16`; `lambda_cs` converges slowest). Native: 5.4e-6, 5.6e-6, 5.4e-6, 4.0e-5, 1.3e-5, 1.2e-3.
- `test_exact_hvp_differentiates_the_force_with_the_same_gauge_flag[True|False]`: at the initial guess of the 3D solovev case (`fsqz` large, so the flags select different forces), the exact HVP matches central differences of `get_forces` evaluated with the same flag to 1e-6 relative; the gauge rows are exactly zero with the flag and O(1) without.
- `test_implicit_vjp_matches_the_pinned_gauge_re_solve`: gradient of `sum(r_cc[12, 1, :]**2) + sum(lambda_sc**2)` w.r.t. `rbc(1, +1)`, `zbs(1, -1)`, `rbc(1, 0)` against Richardson-extrapolated central differences (`h = 1e-4`, `h/2`) of the pinned-gauge re-solve:

  | mode | adjoint | Richardson FD (pinned) | rel. error | FD spread h vs h/2 (pinned) | FD spread (native) | native FD vs adjoint |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | `rbc(1,+1)` | -31.780242 | -31.780490 | 7.8e-6 | 5.1e-6 | 2.5e-5 | 3.2e-4 |
  | `zbs(1,-1)` | -8.891567 | -8.891547 | 2.2e-6 | 8.1e-6 | 1.6e-5 | 1.2e-3 |
  | `rbc(1,0)` | 10.249372 | 10.249315 | 5.6e-6 | 7.4e-7 | 3.0e-5 | 4.7e-4 |

  The test asserts agreement to 1e-4 and FD spread below 1e-4. The native-gauge finite differences are not reproducible below 2e-5 to 3e-5 between step sizes, and their extrapolated values sit 3e-4 to 1.2e-3 from the adjoint: with the free gauge there is no derivative at that level to compare against.

Cost and accuracy of the pinned solve, same case:

- force evaluations to convergence: 1405 native, 1405 pinned; `mhd_energy` 1.2810136e-3 both (reference at mpol 8, ntor 6: 1.2810131e-3);
- truncation error against a native `mpol 8, ntor 6` solve of the same boundary, max over the low modes: `r_cc` 4.04e-4 pinned vs 4.05e-4 native, `r_ss` 3.93e-4 vs 3.94e-4, `z_sc` 3.47e-4 vs 3.47e-4, `z_cs` 3.54e-4 vs 3.54e-4, `lambda_sc` 4.47e-3 vs 4.48e-3, `lambda_cs` 4.33e-3 vs 4.33e-3.

Existing tests: `tests/test_autodiff.py`, `test_geometry.py` (8 passed, 1 skipped: the no-Enzyme fallback test), `test_hessian.py`, `test_iteration.py`, `test_internal_gradient.py`, `test_preconditioner.py` (31 passed) with the Enzyme-enabled build, `OMP_NUM_THREADS=1`. `pre-commit` (clang-format, ruff, docformatter, pyright) passes on the changed files.

Runtime: `test_implicit_vjp_matches_the_pinned_gauge_re_solve` takes about 60 s, of which the adjoint GMRES solve (`rtol 1e-8`, restart 200) at ns 25, mpol 5, ntor 4 is the bulk; the 13 re-solves take under 2 s each.

**<sub><sub>![P2 Badge](https://img.shields.io/badge/P2-yellow?style=flat)</sub></sub>  Exclude capped modes from the prescribed gauge set**

When `mpol_geometry <= 1` or `ntor_geometry < ntor`, `maskGeometryAbove()` zeros the excluded R/Z modes, including these interior and boundary `z_cs` entries, so the forward result is independent of the corresponding boundary coefficients. --- inline thread on vmecpp/vmec/pybind11/pybind_vmec.cc:655-657 ---
> chatgpt-codex-connector[bot], 2026-09-12 15:29 UTC:
**<sub><sub>![P2 Badge](https://img.shields.io/badge/P2-yellow?style=flat)</sub></sub>  Match the false flag to Evaluate's effective gauge decision**

For `always_fix_m1_gauge=False`, `Evaluate()` still fixes the gauge whenever the newly computed `fsqz < 1e-6` (or `iter2 < 2`), whereas this call passes `false` directly as `fix_m1_gauge`. Consequently, on a converged native-gauge model the exact product contains nonzero gauge rows even though the force returned by `evaluate(..., False)` has those rows zeroed, so it is not the advertised Jacobian of that force. Compute and pass the same effective threshold-dependent decision used by `IdealMhdModel::update`.

--- inline thread on vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec.cc:792-795 ---
> chatgpt-codex-connector[bot], 2026-09-12 15:29 UTC:
**<sub><sub>![P2 Badge](https://img.shields.io/badge/P2-yellow?style=flat)</sub></sub>  Re-pin the gauge when enabling it after a hot restart**

`VmecModel.create(..., initial_state)` calls `InitializeRadial()` before Python can set `always_fix_m1_gauge`, so this branch is skipped during the only initialization of a single-resolution hot restart. Setting the property and calling `solve()` then merely zeroes the gauge force and preserves the restart state's interior gauge rather than deriving it from the new boundary, contrary to the fixed-gauge contract. Re-pin when the property is enabled or allow the flag to be supplied to `create()`.

PR #855 [OPEN] autodiff: reuse the forward solve's model in the reverse callback
################################################################################
author: jurasic-pf | opened: 2026-09-12 16:01 UTC
branch: proximafusion:jurasic/autodiff-reverse-reuses-forward -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/855
files (2): src/vmecpp/autodiff.py +118 -8, tests/test_autodiff_cache.py (added) +166 -0

--- description ---
# autodiff: reuse the forward solve's model in the reverse callback

## Scope

`DifferentiableVmec._backward_callback` re-solved the equilibrium from scratch
on every call, so a single `jax.value_and_grad` paid for two nonlinear solves.
Any solver failure, or a non-converged GMRES adjoint solve (`info != 0`),
raised inside the JAX callback, which aborts an optimizer's line search rather
than letting it backtrack.

This change only touches `_backward_callback`'s model acquisition and adds an
opt-in failure mode; it does not change the adjoint algorithm itself (that is
the direct-solve branch stacked on top).

## Change

- Added `_ModelCache`, a small LRU cache (`collections.OrderedDict`-backed)
  from `boundary.tobytes()` to the converged `VmecModel`. `_solve_model_cached`
  looks the model up before falling back to `_solve_model`.
- `DifferentiableVmec` gained `cache_size: int = 4` and
  `on_failure: Literal["raise", "nan"] = "raise"`, threaded through
  `make_solver(vmec_input, *, cache_size=4, on_failure="raise")`. Both
  `_forward_callback` and `_backward_callback` now go through the shared
  per-solver cache, so the model produced by a forward call is available to
  the backward call `jax.custom_vjp` invokes for the same primal value.
- With `on_failure="nan"`, a forward solver exception or a backward adjoint
  exception is caught, reported with `warnings.warn`, and replaced by an
  all-NaN output/cotangent of the expected shape, instead of propagating out
  of the `jax.pure_callback`. Defaults are unchanged
  (`cache_size=4`, `on_failure="raise"`), so existing callers see no behavior
  change unless they opt in.

## Tests and measurements

New file `tests/test_autodiff_cache.py`:
- `test_backward_reuses_the_cached_forward_model`: after a forward call, the
  backward call for the same boundary makes zero additional `_solve_model`
  calls (monkeypatched counter).
- `test_cache_miss_falls_back_to_a_fresh_solve`: a different boundary triggers
  its own solve.
- `test_cache_evicts_the_least_recently_used_entry`: with `cache_size=1`, a
  second boundary evicts the first, and a subsequent backward call for the
  evicted boundary re-solves.
- `test_cache_size_must_be_positive`, `test_on_failure_must_be_a_known_literal`:
  input validation.
- `test_on_failure_nan_warns_and_returns_nan_on_solver_failure` /
  `..._on_adjoint_failure`: a monkeypatched failure in the solve or the
  adjoint VJP is turned into a `UserWarning` plus a NaN-filled array of the
  correct shape.
- `test_on_failure_raise_is_the_default_and_propagates`: the default still
  raises.

All of `tests/test_autodiff.py`, `tests/test_geometry.py`,
`tests/test_hessian.py`, and the new file pass against an Enzyme-enabled
build (19 passed, 1 skipped — the skip is `has_exact_force_jacobian`-gated
and unrelated to this change).

**Wall time**, CTH-like fixed-boundary case, `ns=25`, `ftol=1e-14`, `ncurr=0`
with the converged `ncurr=1` iota profile prescribed as an 8th-order power
series (`ai`), single-core (`OMP_NUM_THREADS=1`), 3 repetitions of
`jax.value_and_grad` on `sum(r_cc**2)`:

| | run 1 | run 2 | run 3 | median |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| before (backward re-solves) | 49.79 s | 49.05 s | 40.41 s | 49.05 s |
| after (backward reuses cache) | 37.67 s | 52.28 s | 49.38 s | 49.38 s |

At this resolution the equilibrium solve itself takes about 2 s, while the
GMRES adjoint solve (unchanged by this branch) takes on the order of 40-50 s,
so removing the redundant equilibrium solve yields a small (roughly 4%)
reduction of the total gradient wall time here; the run-to-run variance in
GMRES iteration count dominates the measured spread. The absolute win grows
in configurations where re-solving costs more relative to the adjoint (e.g.
more multigrid steps, tighter `ftol`, more line-search evaluations per
gradient in an outer optimizer that calls the forward pass repeatedly before
ever asking for a gradient). Reducing the adjoint's own cost is addressed by
the direct-solve branch stacked on top of this one.

## Open issues

- The cache holds full `VmecModel` C++ objects; `cache_size` bounds the count
  but not per-model memory, which scales with `ns * mpol * (ntor + 1)`. Left
  at a default of 4; callers doing large sweeps
  over many boundaries in one process may want a smaller `cache_size`.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)

> chatgpt-codex-connector[bot], 2026-09-12 16:30 UTC:
**<sub><sub>![P1 Badge](https://img.shields.io/badge/P1-orange?style=flat)</sub></sub>  Include the VMEC input in the model-cache key**

When a caller changes any non-boundary field of the mutable `vmec_input` after an evaluation (for example `phiedge`, a pressure/current profile, or an iteration setting) and evaluates the same boundary again, this lookup returns the model built from the old input even though a fresh C++ template was passed. Before this change every callback solved against the current input; now both values and VJPs can silently describe a stale equilibrium. Include the template state in the key, invalidate the cache when it changes, or snapshot the input at construction so mutation cannot imply an update.

PR #856 [OPEN] autodiff: expose the flat state layout as a public StateLayout
################################################################################
author: jurasic-pf | opened: 2026-09-12 16:01 UTC
branch: proximafusion:jurasic/autodiff-state-layout -> jurasic/autodiff-reverse-reuses-forward
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/856
files (2): src/vmecpp/autodiff.py +123 -12, tests/test_autodiff_state_layout.py (added) +148 -0

--- description ---
# autodiff: expose the flat state layout as a public StateLayout

## Scope

The flat solver state (`VmecModel.get_state()`/`get_forces()`) is only
accessible through private helpers (`_span_slices`, `_interior_and_boundary`,
`_structural_nullfree_interior`, `_boundary_from_state_vjp`,
`_cpp_geometry_flat`, `_solve_model`), so downstream code has no supported way
to ask which flat index belongs to which coefficient span, surface, or mode.

## Change

- Added a public, frozen `StateLayout` dataclass and `state_layout(model)`
  constructor. `StateLayout` has:
  - `spans: dict[str, slice]` — the same span order as before, named.
  - `surface: dict[str, np.ndarray]` / `mode: dict[str, np.ndarray]` — per-span
    arrays giving each flat entry's surface index and its `(m, n)`-flattened
    mode index within the surface.
  - `boundary` / `interior` — partition the whole state (fixed `r`/`z` at the
    last surface vs. everything else).
  - `structural_zero` / `gauge` / `solved` — partition `interior`:
    - `structural_zero`: DOFs with both a zero Hessian row and column (no
      force depends on them and they produce no force) — unconstrained
      gauge/parity modes.
    - `gauge`: the m=1 constraint-gauge entries zeroed by
      `FourierForces::zeroZForceForM1` (`z_cs` under `lthreed`, `z_cc` under
      `lasym`). Their force row is identically zero, but unlike
      `structural_zero`, their Hessian *column* is not: the state entry still
      feeds the m=1 constraint coupling (`M1Constraint`) that other rows
      depend on.
    - `solved`: what `_structural_nullfree_interior` returned before this
      change, i.e. the DOFs the adjoint system is actually solved for, with
      the gauge rows now split out by name/mode instead of left folded into
      the general row/column probe. `gauge` rows already fail the probe's
      "row nonzero" test (since the force row is exactly zero), so they were
      already excluded from the old `nullfree` set; this change only gives
      that subset a name and an explicit, structural (not probed)
      derivation.
- `_span_slices` and `_interior_and_boundary` are now thin wrappers, kept for
  the existing private call sites (`_boundary_from_state_vjp`,
  `_implicit_boundary_vjp`, and the tests that probe them directly).
- The module docstring gained a "State layout" section documenting the state
  <-> `Geometry` correspondence: span order and which spans exist for a given
  `lthreed`/`lasym` combination, the `(ns, mpol, ntor + 1)` surface-major block
  layout matching `vmecpp.Geometry`/`get_geometry()`, and the `sqrt(2)` scaling
  of `m, n != 0` modes relative to the external combined basis.
- `StateLayout` and `state_layout` are added to `__all__`.

## Tests and measurements

New file `tests/test_autodiff_state_layout.py`, parameterized over a 2D
(solovev) and a 3D model (the same construction
`tests/test_autodiff.py::_small_3d_input` uses, so the `r_ss`/`z_cs`/`lambda_cs`
blocks and the `z_cs` gauge rows are exercised):

- `test_index_sets_partition_the_state`: `boundary ⊔ interior` covers the full
  state with no overlap, and `structural_zero ⊔ gauge ⊔ solved` covers
  `interior` with no overlap, for both the 2D and 3D cases.
- `test_solved_matches_the_previous_structural_nullfree_interior`: `solved`
  equals `_structural_nullfree_interior(model, interior)` minus `gauge`, i.e.
  the previously-returned interior set, now split into gauge and solved.
- `test_gauge_is_empty_in_two_dimensions`: the 2D case has no
  `lthreed`/`lasym` blocks, so there is nothing for `zeroZForceForM1` to zero.
- `test_gauge_force_rows_are_zero`: in the 3D case, `gauge` is nonempty and
  the raw force at those indices is exactly zero (to `1e-12`).
- `test_spans_partition_the_flat_state`: the named spans tile the flat state
  with no gaps or overlaps.
- `test_surface_and_mode_indices_agree_with_the_span_shape`: `surface`/`mode`
  arrays have the right shape and range for each span.

All of `tests/test_autodiff.py`, `tests/test_autodiff_cache.py` (from the
stacked branch below this one), `tests/test_autodiff_state_layout.py`,
`tests/test_geometry.py`, and `tests/test_hessian.py` pass: 27 passed,
1 skipped (Enzyme-gated), against an Enzyme-enabled build.

## Open issues

- `state_layout`'s `n_probe`/`tol`/`seed` defaults match
  `_structural_nullfree_interior`'s; a caller who wants a different
  probe budget for a larger `mpol`/`ntor` can already pass them through, but
  there is no guidance yet on how to size them for a much larger case (this
  is inherited from the pre-existing probing, not new to this change).
- `gauge` is derived from `zeroZForceForM1`'s `(m=1, all n)` rule read off the
  C++ source rather than from a Hessian probe, so it is exact but tied to
  that specific constraint; if a future constraint changes which entries are
  gauge-fixed, this derivation needs a matching update.

PR #864 [OPEN] Extrapolate the full-grid ends from the interior alone
################################################################################
author: CharlesCNorton | opened: 2026-09-20 16:38 UTC
branch: CharlesCNorton:full-grid-end-extrapolation -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/864
files (2): vmecpp/vmec/output_quantities/output_quantities.cc +48 -81, vmecpp/vmec/output_quantities/output_quantities_test.cc +40 -0

--- description ---
**Change** - the axis and boundary entries of every extrapolated full-grid output now come from interior surfaces only, through one helper `ExtrapolateFullGridEnds` used by `ExtrapolateBSubS`, `ComputeJxBOutputFileContents` and `ComputeIntermediateThreed1FirstTableQuantities`. At `ns = 3`, where a two-point stencil has one interior surface to read, both ends take that value.

**Cause** - each site wrote both ends in one pass, the axis as `2 f[1] - f[2]` and the boundary as `2 f[ns-2] - f[ns-3]`. At `NS_MIN` those stencils overlap: index 0 and index `ns - 3` are the same surface, so the axis was formed from an `f[2]` that is the boundary entry, still zero, and the boundary was then formed from the `f[0]` the same pass had just written, cancelling to exactly zero.

**Evidence** - `solovev` at `ns_array = [3]` and `ftol = 1e-12` converges, and the per-surface maxima of `bsubs_full` are `3.732109e-02`, `1.866055e-02`, `0.000000e+00`: the axis exactly twice the single interior surface, the edge exactly zero. `jdotb` is `[-35470.86, -17735.43, 0]`, `bdotb` `[0.0804, 0.0402, 0]`, `bdotgradv` `[0.0990, 0.0495, 0]`, `equif` `[-0.0012, -0.0006, 0]`, and `bsubsmns_full` `[0.0356, 0.0178, 0]`. The same case at `ns = 4` gives `1.809e-02` to `1.904e-02` and at `ns = 5` `1.798e-02` to `1.891e-02`, monotone with a non-zero edge.

**Tests** - `EndsAreIndependentAtTheSmallestRadialResolution` in `output_quantities_test`, pinning `ns = 3` to the interior value at both ends and `ns = 5` to the two-point extrapolations.

**Scope** - for `ns >= 4` the arithmetic and its order are unchanged, so no reference value moves; `ns = 3` is reachable as a first multigrid step and as `NS_MIN`.

PR #877 [OPEN] Expose the half-grid fields and the enclosed current to the Python-driven solve
################################################################################
author: CharlesCNorton | opened: 2026-09-22 17:50 UTC
branch: CharlesCNorton:expose-half-grid-fields -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/877
files (5): docs/examples_overview.md +1 -0, examples/self_consistent_bootstrap_current.py (added) +265 -0, vmecpp/vmec/pybind11/pybind_vmec.cc +100 -0, tests/test_half_grid_fields.py (added) +137 -0, tests/test_self_consistent_bootstrap_current.py (added) +76 -0

--- description ---
## Change

`VmecModel` gains two accessors:

- `curr_h`: the enclosed toroidal current that `ncurr = 1` prescribes on the half grid, in the units of wout `buco`. It can be read and set between evaluations, and every evaluation makes `buco` equal to it.
- `half_grid_fields()`: the fields of the last evaluation on the half grid. These are `sqrt(g)`, `B^theta`, `B^zeta`, `B_theta` and `B_zeta` on the solver's angular grid with its averaging weights, and the profiles `buco`, `bvco`, `iota`, `phip` and `vp`.

With these, a `solve_equilibrium` callback can read the iterating equilibrium and set the next current profile.

`examples/self_consistent_bootstrap_current.py` uses this for SIMSOPT's Redl closure on the quasi-helical warm start. Every 25 iterations, the callback moves the current halfway to the Redl current of the iterating field. The solve ends when the forces converge and the closure changes the current by less than 1e-3 of its maximum.

## Cause

A closure for the current profile needs the iterating field and a way to set the current between iterations. The review of #876 asked for both through the callback or the Python API, with the closure kept outside the solver.

## Evidence

The quasi-helical warm start at ns = 50, with n_e = 1e19 (1 - s^5) m^-3, T_e = T_i = 4 (1 - s) keV and a volume-averaged beta of 2%:

| run | net current | iota mid | iota edge | Redl mismatch |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| zero current | 0 A | -1.0609 | -1.1187 | 1.00 |
| closure through the callback | -11672 A | -1.0016 | -1.0089 | 2.0e-3 |

The mismatch is the measure of SIMSOPT's `VmecRedlBootstrapMismatch`. It is recomputed from the wout spectrum of a separate solve that uses the final current profile. The mismatch falls as the radial grid is refined: 1.6e-2 at ns = 16, 7.2e-3 at 25 and 2.0e-3 at 50. After the starting equilibrium, the closure takes 1346 force iterations, and the whole example runs in 11 s.

## Tests

- `test_half_grid_fields.py` covers the following, on a symmetric and an asymmetric case:
  - `buco` and `bvco` are the averages of `B_theta` and `B_zeta`, `vp` is `signgs` times the average of `sqrt(g)`, and `iota` is `chi'/phi'`.
  - The fields equal the wout at convergence.
  - After `curr_h` is set, the next evaluation gives `buco = curr_h`, and the setter rejects `ncurr = 0` and a wrong length.
  - The example's reflection onto the full poloidal grid is correct.
- `test_self_consistent_bootstrap_current.py` runs the example at ns = 16 and 25 and checks it against SIMSOPT's mismatch measure.
- The Python test suite and pre-commit pass.

## Scope

The only change to VMEC++ is the two `VmecModel` accessors. The closure and the iteration of the current are in the example.

PR #878 [OPEN] Shorten time steps that would make flux surfaces cross
################################################################################
author: CharlesCNorton | opened: 2026-09-22 19:06 UTC
branch: CharlesCNorton:jacobian-safe-step -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/878
files (18): src/vmecpp/__init__.py +7 -0, vmecpp/common/vmec_indata/vmec_indata.cc +15 -0, vmecpp/common/vmec_indata/vmec_indata.h +5 -0, vmecpp/test_data/BUILD.bazel +8 -0, vmecpp/test_data/constellaration_nfp5.json (added) +485 -0, vmecpp/vmec/handover_storage/handover_storage.h +5 -0, vmecpp/vmec/ideal_mhd_model/BUILD.bazel +10 -0, vmecpp/vmec/ideal_mhd_model/ideal_mhd_model.cc +125 -1, vmecpp/vmec/ideal_mhd_model/ideal_mhd_model.h +33 -1, vmecpp/vmec/ideal_mhd_model/step_limit_kernel.h (added) +66 -0, vmecpp/vmec/ideal_mhd_model/step_limit_kernel_test.cc (added) +141 -0, vmecpp/vmec/pybind11/pybind_vmec.cc +18 -6, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/BUILD.bazel +1 -0, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec.cc +162 -6, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec.h +40 -0, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec_test.cc +91 -0, vmecpp/vmec/vmec_constants/vmec_algorithm_constants.h +9 -0, tests/test_jacobian_safe_step.py (added) +34 -0

--- description ---
## Change

`jacobian_safe_step` (default false) shortens every time step at whose end the half-grid Jacobian `tau` would fall below 1% of its value at the start of the step, at some grid point.

After each step, the evaluation compares `tau` with its value at the start of the step. `tau` is a quadratic form in the geometry, so along the step it is exactly quadratic in the step length. Where `tau` has fallen below the bound, this gives the largest fraction of the step that keeps it. The state then moves back along the step to that fraction, the velocity is scaled by the same fraction, and the model is evaluated again. A backup taken at the end of the step moves to its start, the last evaluated state.

Steps that keep the bound are unchanged, and so is the iteration with the flag off. The C++ solve and the Python iteration of `solve_equilibrium` take the same steps.

## Cause

On main, a step that makes the Jacobian change sign is detected only after it is taken. The step is undone to the last backup and retried with a time step 10% smaller. After 75 of those, the run fails with `JACOBIAN_75_TIMES_BAD`.

## Evidence

The test set is 3000 random boundaries of the ConStellaration training set, run with the VMEC++ inputs of the ConStellaration forward model's low-fidelity preset: ns = 25, then 71, at time step 0.7. On main, 100 of them fail:

| failure on main | count | with `jacobian_safe_step` |
|---|---|---|
| `JACOBIAN_75_TIMES_BAD` | 33 | 30 converge, 3 fail |
| iteration limit | 28 | 28 fail |
| in the first iterations | 39 | 39 fail |

No run with the flag ends at 75 restarts. Of the 3 that still fail, 2 reach the iteration limit and 1 fails in the first iterations.

The 23 rescued boundaries converge on main at time step 0.5. Against main at 0.35, their equilibria with the flag at 0.7 differ more than main's at 0.5:

| median relative difference, 23 boundaries | `rmnc` | `zmns` | `iotaf` |
|---|---|---|---|
| main at 0.5 against main at 0.35 | 1.2e-4 | 8.6e-4 | 4.8e-4 |
| flag at 0.7 against main at 0.35 | 3.2e-4 | 2.4e-3 | 1.6e-3 |

On the test boundary, main's own converged state moves steadily with the time step. The move is in the m = 1, n = 1 harmonic of Z, and tightening `ftol` from 1e-9 to 1e-15 leaves it unchanged. Main at 0.65, the largest time step at which main converges there, lands as far from main at 0.35 as the flag does at 0.7:

| relative difference to main at 0.35, test boundary, `ftol` = 1e-11 | `rmnc` | `zmns` | `iotaf` |
|---|---|---|---|
| main at 0.5 | 1.2e-4 | 1.2e-3 | 7.3e-5 |
| main at 0.65 | 3.1e-4 | 2.9e-3 | 2.5e-4 |
| flag at 0.7 | 3.1e-4 | 2.9e-3 | 2.5e-4 |

240 boundaries that converge on main were also run with the flag:

- All 240 converge.
- 233 take exactly the same iterations as on main, because none of their steps reaches the bound.
- The other 7 take 1 to 15 restarts on main and none with the flag. Their edge iota agrees with main's to 1.4e-4.
- When no step reaches the bound, the check costs 3% of the run time.

## Tests

- `step_limit_kernel_test` checks the root finder, the retained fraction at the binding point of a shortened step, and `tau` along a step against its quadratic form to 1e-12.
- `vmec_test` uses a ConStellaration boundary whose time steps at 0.7 cross flux surfaces. Without the flag it fails with `JACOBIAN_75_TIMES_BAD`. With the flag it converges, to the same state on one thread and on four.
- `vmec_test` also runs the same boundary at time step 0.35 and `cth_like_fixed_bdy`, where no step reaches the bound. With the flag, their iterations and states are unchanged bit for bit.
- `test_jacobian_safe_step.py` runs the Python iteration on the same boundary. It fails without the flag and converges with it, in the same number of iterations as the C++ solve.
- The C++ and Python test suites and pre-commit pass.

## Scope

The limit acts only on time steps, so failures in the first iterations are unchanged. It does not act on restarts triggered by residual growth. The retained fraction is the constant `kJacobianRetainedFraction`.

PR #884 [OPEN] Retry a bad initial Jacobian from a three-surface mesh
################################################################################
author: CharlesCNorton | opened: 2026-09-25 15:59 UTC
branch: CharlesCNorton:jacob-off-retry -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/884
files (3): vmecpp/test_data/constellaration_bad_initial_jacobian.json (added) +201 -0, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec.cc +13 -5, tests/test_init.py +29 -0

--- description ---
**Change** - a bad initial Jacobian that the new axis guess does not fix no longer ends the run: `SolveEquilibriumLoop` returns to `Vmec::run`, which leaves the multigrid loop and runs the second pass of its `jacob_off` loop, a solve at ns = 3 to `ftol = 1e-4` followed by `ns_array`. A bad Jacobian in that pass is reported as before. The logger numbers the stages of the pass from 0.

**Cause** - the `jacob_off` loop, VMEC's `jacob_off = 1` retry, could not run. It needs `status_ == BAD_JACOBIAN` after the multigrid loop, but inside the loop that status had already returned an error, or given up under `return_outputs_even_if_not_converged`. Had the pass run, `logger_.BeginStage(-1, ...)` would have read `stages_[-1]`. educational_VMEC leaves its multigrid loop on the flag and repeats it with ns = 3 inserted (`vmec.f90`), and PARVMEC does the same (`runvmec.f`).

**Evidence** - the stellarator-symmetric boundaries of one shard of the ConStellaration dataset (`data/train-00002-of-00003.parquet`), with the VMEC++ input of its forward model's low-fidelity preset (ns = 25 then 71, time step 0.7). Of the 23,536 rows marked with a forward-model error, 2,896 fail in their first iterations on main; of 1,000 unmarked rows, none. On 400 of the 2,896:

| outcome | main | here |
| --- | --- | --- |
| fails in the first iterations | 400 | 378 |
| converges | 0 | 14 |
| reaches the iteration limit | 0 | 6 |
| `JACOBIAN_75_TIMES_BAD` | 0 | 2 |

The 14 converged equilibria agree with main run on the same schedule with ns = 3 given first, to 2e-9 in `rmnc` and `zmns` for 13 of them and 7e-5 for the last. A boundary that still fails takes about 0.1 s longer. Built the same way, main and this branch give the same iteration counts and bit-identical `rmnc`, `zmns`, `lmns` and `iotaf` on 36 of the unmarked boundaries.

The non-stellarator-symmetric and free-boundary paths, started from the same boundary at 8 threads. Each run that converges here equals main run with ns = 3 given first in `rmnc`, `zmns`, `iotaf`, the volume and `wb`, and under `lasym` in `rmns` and `zmnc`:

| input | main | here |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `lasym`, asymmetric part zero | fails in the first iterations | converges, 971 iterations |
| `lasym`, `rbs(3,0)` = 3e-4, `zbc(3,0)` = 2.25e-4 | fails in the first iterations | converges, 970 iterations |
| `lasym`, `rbs(3,0)` = 1e-3, `zbc(3,0)` = 7.5e-4 | fails in the first iterations | converges, 968 iterations |
| free boundary, `cth_like_free_bdy` at ns = 25 started from this boundary scaled to R = 0.78 | fails in the first iterations | converges, 602 iterations |
| the same under `lasym` with the coils of `cth_like_free_bdy_asym` | fails in the first iterations | converges, 4476 iterations |

Through the `lasym` path the retry reproduces the symmetric result: at 1, 2, 4 and 8 threads it takes the same 971 iterations, `rmnc` and `zmns` agree within 3.2e-12, and every asymmetric coefficient stays below 3e-16. A boundary that also fails at ns = 3 is not rescued: `cth_like_free_bdy` with its m >= 2 coefficients scaled by 8 fails in the first iterations on main, here, and on main with ns = 3 given first.

**Tests** - `test_bad_initial_jacobian_is_retried_from_three_surfaces` in `tests/test_init.py` runs `constellaration_bad_initial_jacobian.json`, ConStellaration boundary `DCWhGgAc7UMiZ8VC3Lx34BQ` at ns = 25, which fails in its first iterations on main and converges here, then runs it through the `lasym` path with a zero asymmetric part and holds `rmnc` and `zmns` to the symmetric result within 2e-11.

**Scope** - runs whose initial Jacobian stays bad after the axis guess; every other run takes the path it took before. The dataset is under the MIT license.

PR #892 [OPEN] Read CSV coefficient files that begin with a UTF-8 byte order mark
################################################################################
author: CharlesCNorton | opened: 2026-09-25 20:08 UTC
branch: CharlesCNorton:csv-byte-order-mark -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/892
files (2): vmecpp/common/composed_types_lib/composed_types_lib.cc +10 -2, vmecpp/common/composed_types_lib/composed_types_lib_test.cc +28 -0

--- description ---
**Change** - `CurveRZFourierFromCsv` and `SurfaceRZFourierFromCsv` drop a UTF-8 byte order mark from the start of the header line before comparing it.

**Cause** - programs that save CSV as UTF-8, Excel's "CSV UTF-8" among them, write the bytes `EF BB BF` ahead of the first line. `StripAsciiWhitespace` leaves them in place, so such a file was rejected with `header line 'n,raxis_c,zaxis_s,raxis_s,zaxis_c' not found`, or its boundary counterpart, as a CRLF file was before #888.

**Tests** - `CheckReadCoefficientsFromCsvWithByteOrderMark` in `composed_types_lib_test` reads an axis file with the mark and LF line endings and a boundary file with the mark and CRLF line endings; on main it fails at the axis header with that message.

**Scope** - the two CSV readers, whose callers are `surface_geometry_mockup` and the tests. A file without the mark reads as before.

################################################################################
PR #894 [OPEN] Return an error for a thread count below 1 instead of aborting
################################################################################
author: CharlesCNorton | opened: 2026-09-25 21:54 UTC
branch: CharlesCNorton:thread-count-checks -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/894
files (3): vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec.cc +8 -0, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec_test.cc +17 -0, vmecpp/vmec/vmec_standalone/vmec_standalone.cc +9 -1

--- description ---
**Change** - `Vmec::FromIndata`, through which both `vmecpp::run` overloads build the solver, returns `kInvalidArgument` for a `max_threads` below 1, naming it, before the constructor hands the count to `FlowControl`. `vmec_standalone` parses its thread count with `absl::SimpleAtoi` and exits 1 with a usage message when the argument is not a positive integer, where it read it with `std::atoi`.

**Cause** - `get_max_threads` in `flow_control.cc` holds the count to `CHECK_GT(max_threads.value(), 0)`, which aborts the process. `vmecpp.run` in Python checks the count first and raises `RuntimeError`, but nothing below it does: `_vmecpp.run(..., max_threads=0)` took the Python interpreter down, and `vmec_standalone` aborted for 0, for a negative count and for an argument `std::atoi` reads as 0, such as `abc`. `std::atoi` also read `1x` as 1, and its result for a value outside `int` is undefined.

**Evidence** - main and this branch:

| call | main | here |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `vmec_standalone solovev.json 4` | exit 0 | exit 0 |
| `vmec_standalone solovev.json 0`, `-2` or `abc` | exit 134, `Check failed: max_threads.value() > 0` at `flow_control.cc:37` | exit 1, `n_max_threads must be a positive integer, but is '0'` |
| `vmec_standalone solovev.json 1x` or `99999999999` | runs | exit 1, `n_max_threads must be a positive integer, but is '1x'` |
| `_vmecpp.run(indata, max_threads=0)` | the interpreter aborts at the same check | `AttributeError: The number of threads must be >= 1, but is 0. To use all available threads, leave max_threads unset.` |

**Tests** - `CheckRunRejectsAThreadCountBelowOne` in `vmec_test` requires `kInvalidArgument` from `vmecpp::run` for 0 and -2; on main the test process aborts at the check.

**Scope** - the check in `get_max_threads` stays for a `Vmec` constructed directly. `vmecpp.run` in Python rejected these counts already and is unchanged.

################################################################################
PR #897 [OPEN] Size the OpenMP teams to the threads the runtime grants
################################################################################
author: CharlesCNorton | opened: 2026-09-26 18:48 UTC
branch: CharlesCNorton:vacuum-thread-team -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/897
files (7): vmecpp/common/util/util.cc +29 -0, vmecpp/common/util/util.h +10 -0, vmecpp/vmec/handover_storage/handover_storage.h +4 -0, vmecpp/vmec/ideal_mhd_model/ideal_mhd_model.cc +35 -24, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec.cc +42 -16, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec.h +6 -4, tests/test_init.py +55 -0

--- description ---
**Change** - `InitializeRadial` sizes the radial team to the threads OpenMP grants a region that requests it (`GrantedThreads`), and `SetupVacuumSolvers` sizes the NESTOR vacuum team to the threads granted a region nested in that radial team (`GrantedNestedThreads`), rebuilding the vacuum solvers when that size changes between multigrid steps. A team that still comes up short makes its region return a `ResourceExhausted` status; the vacuum region used to abort the process with `CHECK_EQ(omp_get_num_threads(), m_vac_num_threads_)`.

**Cause** - OpenMP grants a parallel region fewer threads than its `num_threads` clause asks for under `OMP_THREAD_LIMIT`, and a nested region fewer once the active levels run out. The radial and tangential partitionings were built for the requested counts, so the radial solve left the surfaces of the missing threads unevaluated and failed, and the vacuum solve aborted the process, and with it the Python interpreter.

**Evidence** - `OMP_THREAD_LIMIT=2`, `vmec_standalone`:

| case | main | here |
| --- | --- | --- |
| solovev, 4 threads | `The solver failed during the first iterations` | converges; `wout` equal bit for bit to the 2-thread run |
| cth_like_free_bdy, 2 threads | aborts: `Check failed: omp_get_num_threads() == m_vac_num_threads_ (1 vs. 2)` | converges; volume within 9e-14 and `rmnc` within 4e-13 of the run with a 2-thread vacuum team |

Without a limit, solovev on 2 and 4 threads and cth_like_free_bdy on 2 threads give the same `wout` as main bit for bit.

**Tests** - `test_run_under_an_openmp_thread_limit` in `tests/test_init.py` runs both cases in a subprocess under `OMP_THREAD_LIMIT=2`; on main it fails.

**Scope** - Without a thread limit or an enclosing parallel region every team gets what it requests, and the partitioning is unchanged.

PR #902 [OPEN] Accept the mgrid mode N that simsopt writes and LIBSTELL assigns to mgrid files without a mode
################################################################################
author: CharlesCNorton | opened: 2026-09-27 11:20 UTC
branch: CharlesCNorton:mgrid-mode-none -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/902
files (2): src/vmecpp/__init__.py +2 -2, tests/test_init.py +53 -0

--- description ---
**Change** - `MgridModeType` accepts `N` besides `R`, `S` and the empty string, and its docstring lists the modes in the order of the literal, `[Raw, Scaled, None, Unset]`; it read `[Scaled, Raw, Unset]`.

**Cause** - simsopt's mgrid writer (`MGrid.write_netcdf`, `simsopt/field/mgrid.py`) sets `mgrid_mode = 'N'`, and LIBSTELL's `read_mgrid_nc` (`mgrid_mod.f`) sets it to `'N'` for a file without the variable, which `wrout.f` writes into the wout. `VmecWOut` validated `mgrid_mode` against `R`, `S` and the empty string. The C++ mgrid reader copies the mode into the wout and reads it nowhere else.

**Evidence** - `cth_like_free_bdy` against an mgrid file written from the `coils.cth_like` response table with `mgrid_mode` set to `N`: on main, `vmecpp.run` raises a `ValidationError` on `mgrid_mode` when it builds the `VmecWOut`; here the wout reports `N`, and a wout file saved from it loads back with `N`.

**Tests** - `test_free_boundary_run_with_mgrid_mode_none` in `tests/test_init.py`, which fails on main with that `ValidationError`. It writes its mgrid file from the coils response table and reads no git-lfs data.

**Scope** - `MgridModeType` only.

PR #903 [OPEN] Return NESTOR's boundary vacuum field with return_vacuum_field
################################################################################
author: CharlesCNorton | opened: 2026-09-27 11:40 UTC
branch: CharlesCNorton:return-vacuum-field -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/903
files (6): src/vmecpp/__init__.py +87 -3, vmecpp/common/vmec_indata/vmec_indata.cc +15 -0, vmecpp/common/vmec_indata/vmec_indata.h +6 -0, vmecpp/common/vmec_indata/vmec_indata_test.cc +3 -0, vmecpp/vmec/pybind11/pybind_vmec.cc +18 -0, tests/test_init.py +32 -0

--- description ---
**Change** - `VmecInput.return_vacuum_field` (default false) makes `vmecpp.run` fill `VmecOutput.threed1_free_boundary`, a `Threed1FreeBoundary` holding the boundary geometry (`rb`, `zb`, `phib`), the plasma- and vacuum-side pressures when the vacuum field was first established and at convergence (`bsqmhdi`, `bsqvaci`, `bsqmhdf`, `bsqvacf`), the plasma-side field on the boundary (`bredge`, `bpedge`, `bzedge`) and the vacuum field NESTOR computes there (`brv`, `bphiv`, `bzv`), each on the solver's `(nzeta, ntheta)` grid. The flag is a `VmecINDATA` field with the JSON and HDF5 round trips of the others, and the table is bound in pybind11 and registered as a JAX pytree like the other output tables.

**Cause** - `ComputeThreed1FreeBoundary` fills `OutputQuantities::threed1_free_boundary` (Fortran VMEC's `freeb_data`), but neither the struct nor the member was bound, so the vacuum field on the boundary was not reachable from Python.

**Evidence** - `cth_like_free_bdy` against the response table of `coils.cth_like`, with the flag: every array has the shape of `bsqvacf`, and `0.5 (brv^2 + bphiv^2 + bzv^2)` equals `bsqvacf` to 1e-12 relative. Without the flag `threed1_free_boundary` is `None`.

**Tests** - `test_threed1_free_boundary_bindings` in `tests/test_init.py`; `vmec_indata_test` checks the field's default, its HDF5 round trip and its copy; `test_vmec_output_serialization` passes over output tables a run leaves absent.

**Scope** - output only; the solve is unchanged.
