PR #768 [CLOSED, not merged] Warn when a resonant harmonic marks a suppressed island chain
################################################################################
author: CharlesCNorton | opened: 2026-09-01 17:24 UTC | closed: 2026-09-02 10:29 UTC
branch: CharlesCNorton:island-diagnostics -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/768
files (3): vmecpp/vmec/output_quantities/output_quantities.cc +165 -0, vmecpp/vmec/output_quantities/output_quantities.h +26 -0, vmecpp/vmec/output_quantities/output_quantities_test.cc +72 -0

--- description ---
VMEC's nested-surface ansatz cannot open an island, so where the real field would, the resonance shows up instead as a locally enhanced (m, n) harmonic of |B| pinned to its rational surface. `ComputeIslandDiagnostics` scans the rotational transform profile for crossings of the field-period-compatible rationals n/m with n a multiple of nfp, gcd(m, n/nfp) = 1 and m up to a bound of 6 by default, and evaluates the resonant harmonic at each crossing: its amplitude normalized by |B|_00, the median of the same harmonic over the off-resonance surfaces, and the quasi-linear island width estimate 4 sqrt(amplitude / (m |iota'|)) in s.

After the wout is computed, a crossing whose resonant amplitude is at least twice its off-resonance level prints a warning naming the rational, its position, the enhancement factor and the width estimate. The shipped equilibria do cross low-order rationals (cma crosses 4/7, cth_like_fixed_bdy crosses 5/5 and 5/4, li383 crosses 3/5, 3/6 and 3/7), and none of them triggers the warning, so the default suite output is unchanged.

The test runs cma, whose only low-order crossing is 4/7: the default bound of 6 finds nothing, admitting m = 7 finds exactly that crossing at the surface where iotaf crosses 4/7 to 1e-12, and pinning an artificial bump of the (7, 4) harmonic to that surface raises the amplitude past the warning threshold against an unchanged background.

--- comment by jurasic-pf, 2026-09-01 23:00 UTC ---
While this sort of diagnostic may be useful, I see this intentionally out of scope for the vmecpp repo and more on the user to implement.

Even more, I have my doubts that a, B based diagnostics can reliably detect formation of resonant features like islands.Instead I expect formation of near singular currents that create an opposing resonant field to satisfy flux surfaces. Do you have any evidence to prove otherwise? Is there poincare plots or verifications with other solvers that indicate this metric correlates with the actual island width observed for a rational crossing?

--- comment by jurasic-pf, 2026-09-02 07:02 UTC ---
If not I'd rather close the issue.
If there's evidence that this is a good metric for predicting emergence of islands we can merge it as a post processing example, but not as part of the core solver :)

--- comment by CharlesCNorton, 2026-09-02 10:29 UTC ---
No evidence beyond the artificial-bump test. Closing.

PR #770 [CLOSED, not merged] Add the Boozer transform of a converged equilibrium
################################################################################
author: CharlesCNorton | opened: 2026-09-01 21:40 UTC | closed: 2026-09-01 22:17 UTC
branch: CharlesCNorton:boozer-transform -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/770
files (10): src/vmecpp/__init__.py +28 -0, vmecpp/vmec/CMakeLists.txt +1 -0, vmecpp/vmec/boozer/BUILD.bazel (added) +35 -0, vmecpp/vmec/boozer/CMakeLists.txt (added) +5 -0, vmecpp/vmec/boozer/boozer.cc (added) +305 -0, vmecpp/vmec/boozer/boozer.h (added) +75 -0, vmecpp/vmec/boozer/boozer_test.cc (added) +143 -0, vmecpp/vmec/pybind11/BUILD.bazel +1 -0, vmecpp/vmec/pybind11/pybind_vmec.cc +33 -0, tests/test_boozer.py (added) +72 -0

--- description ---
`BoozerTransform` (C++, `vmecpp/vmec/boozer`) and `vmecpp.boozer_transform` (Python) compute the Boozer coordinates of the half-grid flux surfaces of a converged stellarator-symmetric equilibrium from its `wout`: the Boozer spectra of |B|, R, Z, the angle transformation function nu and the Boozer Jacobian, together with G, I and iota per surface and the mode numbers in the `wout` convention.

The Boozer angles are theta_B = theta + lambda + iota nu and zeta_B = zeta + nu, with nu = (w - I lambda) / (G + iota I) and w the periodic part of the magnetic scalar potential on the surface, assembled mode by mode from B_theta and B_zeta. Each spectrum is a direct quadrature over the VMEC angles carrying the Jacobian of the angle map, so the map is never inverted. Geometry is taken to the half grid as booz_xform does, odd-m coefficients interpolated as coefficient / sqrt(s) with the m = 1 quotient extrapolated to the axis on the first half-grid surface, and the Jacobian is reported as (G + iota I) / |B|^2 in the Boozer angles. `jacobian_spread` gives per surface the relative variation of sqrt(g_B) |B|^2, which is a flux function in exact Boozer coordinates and so measures the residual of the transformation.

Verification is `tests/test_boozer.py` and `vmecpp/vmec/boozer:boozer_test`. On `cth_like_fixed_bdy` with mboz = nboz = 8 every surface and mode of `bmnc_b`, `rmnc_b`, `zmns_b`, `numns_b` and `gmnc_b` agrees with booz_xform 0.1.0 to 1e-9 of the largest coefficient of the array (the test is skipped when booz_xform is not installed), and G and I agree with `bvco` and `buco` exactly. The C++ tests check that sqrt(g_B) |B|^2 is a flux function to the force tolerance of the case, that the Jacobian-weighted surface average of |B| is the same in the VMEC and Boozer angles, that an axisymmetric equilibrium has no toroidal Boozer harmonics, and the argument validation. Non-stellarator-symmetric equilibria return `Unimplemented`.

--- comment by jurasic-pf, 2026-09-01 22:17 UTC ---
Hi Charles, unfortunately I don't this vmecpp is the place for this. Theres multiple tools in the stellarator community that solve this problem like booz_xform and interact perfectly fine with vmecpp

PR #798 [CLOSED, not merged] Reject the unimplemented sum_cossq profile names at input validation
################################################################################
author: CharlesCNorton | opened: 2026-09-05 01:08 UTC | closed: 2026-09-08 16:25 UTC
branch: CharlesCNorton:profile-reject-unimplemented -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/798
files (5): README.md +2 -0, vmecpp/common/vmec_indata/vmec_indata.cc +7 -0, vmecpp/common/vmec_indata/vmec_indata_test.cc +21 -0, vmecpp/vmec/profile_parameterization_data/profile_parameterization_data.cc +11 -4, vmecpp/vmec/profile_parameterization_data/profile_parameterization_data.h +6 -1

--- description ---
`sum_cossq_s`, `sum_cossq_sqrts` and `sum_cossq_s_free` are registered in `AllProfileParameterizations()` with `allowedForCurr = true`, so `CheckProfile` accepts them as current profiles. `evalProfileFunction` has no case for any of them: they fall through to `default:`, which writes one line to stderr and returns `0.0` for every `s`.

That is the failure the validation added in #719 was meant to prevent, per its own comment: an unrecognized name "reaches the solver as a zero profile, which converges to a silently wrong equilibrium."

With `ncurr = 1` the consequence is that the prescribed net toroidal current is dropped. `evalCurrProfile(1.0)` returns zero, so the `|edgeCurrent| > eps * curtor` guard in `evalRadialProfiles` fails, `Itor` stays zero, and `currH` is zero on every surface. Running `cth_like_fixed_bdy.json` (`ncurr = 1`, `curtor = 4.322908e+04`) unchanged and then with `pcurr_type = "sum_cossq_s"`:

| | `two_power` (as shipped) | `sum_cossq_s` |
|---|---|---|
| `ier_flag` | 0 | 0 |
| `fsqr` | 9.348e-07 | 9.918e-07 |
| `ctor` | +4.322913e+04 A | -2.554651e-11 A |
| max abs `jcurv` | 1.201671e+04 | 2.284859e-10 |

The run terminates normally and writes a `wout` that reports convergence to a zero-current equilibrium while the input asked for 43.2 kA. The only signal is the stderr line, which carries no error flag and is easily lost through the Python API.

The parameterization table now records whether a name has an evaluator, and `CheckProfile` rejects one that does not, so these inputs fail up front instead of solving the wrong problem. The three names stay in the table and keep their enum values, so implementing them later is a local change. The README already listed them as not implemented; it now also says they are rejected rather than evaluated as zero.

The new test fails on all three names without the validation change and passes with it.

--- comment by CharlesCNorton, 2026-09-08 16:25 UTC ---
Superseded by #832, which implements the three profiles instead of rejecting them.

PR #811 [CLOSED, not merged] Integrate the line-segment profile to the evaluation point, not the next knot
################################################################################
author: CharlesCNorton | opened: 2026-09-06 19:32 UTC | closed: 2026-09-06 21:42 UTC
branch: CharlesCNorton:fix/line-segment-integral -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/811
files (2): vmecpp/vmec/radial_profiles/radial_profiles.cc +19 -24, vmecpp/vmec/radial_profiles/radial_profiles_test.cc +26 -0

--- description ---
`evalLineSegmentIntegrated` builds the last, truncated piece of the integral from the value at the *next knot* rather than the value at the truncation point, so the straight line it integrates has the wrong slope whenever the evaluation point falls between two knots. The result is exact on the knots and wrong in between.

For knots at `0, 0.1, ..., 1.0` and values `1.0, 0.95, 0.86, 0.74, 0.60, 0.46, 0.33, 0.21, 0.12, 0.06, 0.03`:

| s | before | exact |
|---|---|---|
| 0.05 | 4.875000e-02 | 4.937500e-02 |
| 0.25 | 2.280000e-01 | 2.295000e-01 |
| 0.50 | 3.880000e-01 | 3.880000e-01 |
| 0.75 | 4.627500e-01 | 4.638750e-01 |
| 1.00 | 4.845000e-01 | 4.845000e-01 |

`pcurr_type = 'line_segment_ip'` is the only profile that reaches this path. On `cth_like_fixed_bdy` with `ncurr = 1` and the current derivative above, the converged equilibrium differed from educational_VMEC by 1.8e-02 in `iotaf`, 1.6e-02 in `jcurv`, 8.6e-04 in `ctor` and 3.7e-05 in `rmnc`, and by 7.2e-01 in `DCurr`. Every field now agrees below 1e-09 with the iteration count unchanged at 413. The other nine current parameterizations, including `akima_spline_ip` and `cubic_spline_ip`, already agreed and still do.

The walk over the knots also read one past the end of both arrays when the evaluation point lay beyond the last knot, and added that piece twice.

The rewritten loop integrates the interpolant from 0 to x piece by piece, taking each endpoint value from the interpolant itself, which also covers the extrapolation below the first knot and above the last. The test compares it against a fine trapezoidal quadrature of the same interpolant on and between the knots and outside both ends.

`bazel test --config=opt //vmecpp/... //vmecpp_large_cpp_tests/...` and `pytest` pass.

--- comment by CharlesCNorton, 2026-09-06 21:42 UTC ---
Folded into #812, which now fixes both line-segment evaluators together as requested there. Closing.

PR #826 [CLOSED, not merged, draft] Fix missing quasisymmetry re-export causing "Test conda env" job failure
################################################################################
author: Copilot | opened: 2026-09-07 23:47 UTC | closed: 2026-09-10 21:28 UTC

--- description ---
The "Test conda env" job (run 34170619332) failed with `AttributeError: module 'vmecpp.simsopt_compat' has no attribute 'magnetic_field_strength'` (and two similar errors), because `tests/test_qs.py` calls these functions on `vmecpp.simsopt_compat` but they were never actually exposed there.

## Root cause

- `src/vmecpp/_qs.py` defines `magnetic_field_strength`, `quasisymmetry_residuals`, and `quasisymmetry_total`.
- Its docstring states these are "Re-exported from `vmecpp.simsopt_compat`", but the rename commit that introduced this comment never added the corresponding import — the re-export was a no-op comment.

## Fix

- Added the missing import in `src/vmecpp/simsopt_compat.py` to re-export `magnetic_field_strength`, `quasisymmetry_residuals`, and `quasisymmetry_total` from `vmecpp._qs`.

```python
from vmecpp._qs import (  # noqa: F401
    magnetic_field_strength,
    quasisymmetry_residuals,
    quasisymmetry_total,
)
```

- Included `src/vmecpp/_qs.py` and `tests/test_qs.py`, which were part of the same in-flight change but not yet present on this branch, so the re-export has something to target and the fix is exercised by the existing test.

PR #850 [CLOSED, not merged] JAX port of the wout output post processing
################################################################################
author: jurasic-pf | opened: 2026-09-12 15:31 UTC | closed: 2026-09-25 21:29 UTC
branch: proximafusion:jurasic/autodiff-run-wout -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/850
files (5): README.md +29 -0, src/vmecpp/__init__.py +44 -1, src/vmecpp/autodiff.py +49 -2, src/vmecpp/autodiff_wout.py (added) +836 -0, tests/test_autodiff_wout.py (added) +245 -0

--- description ---
# Make `vmecpp.run` differentiable (JAX port of the wout output post processing)

## Scope

`vmecpp.autodiff.make_solver` returns the equilibrium as a `Geometry` in the internal product basis (`r_cc, r_ss, z_sc, z_cs, lambda_sc, lambda_cs` on the full mesh plus the flux profiles). Objectives are written in `wout` quantities, so every user had to re-implement the basis conversion and the output stage in JAX before `jax.grad` was
useful. This change adds that port once, exposes it through `vmecpp.autodiff.run`, and lets `vmecpp.run(input, differentiable=True)` dispatch to it.

The differentiable path covers fixed-boundary, stellarator-symmetric, `ncurr = 0` inputs; the gradient needs a build with `-DVMECPP_ENABLE_ENZYME=ON`.

## Tests and measurements

`tests/test_autodiff_wout.py`, run with `OMP_NUM_THREADS=1 JAX_PLATFORMS=cpu` on the
Enzyme-enabled build.

Validation of `wout_arrays` against `vmecpp.run(...).wout` on the same converged state

CTH-like fixed boundary (`examples/data/cth_like_fixed_bdy.json`, `ncurr = 0`,
`ai = [0.35, 0.15]`, `ns = 15`, `ftol = 1e-16`; `mnmax = 41`, `mnmax_nyq = 315`):

| rmnc | 6.9e-18 | 8.0e-01 | bsupumnc | 4.8e-15 | 3.8e-01 |
| zmns | 6.9e-18 | 1.6e-01 | bsupvmnc | 8.5e-15 | 7.4e-01 |
| lmns | 1.1e-16 | 6.5e-01 | bsubsmns | 1.5e-16 | 6.7e-02 |
| lmns_full | 5.6e-17 | 6.6e-01 | iotas / iotaf | 7.2e-16 / 6.1e-16 | 0.49 / 0.50 |

Further tests: `wout_arrays` with a prescribed `iota_half`; `autodiff.run` against
`vmecpp.run` through the two solver paths (agree to `1e-9` relative on `rmnc, zmns,
bmnc, gmnc, iotaf, aspect, volume_p` at `ftol = 1e-16`); `vmecpp.run(differentiable=True)`
returns a `DifferentiableRun` and rejects `restart_from`; `autodiff.run` under `jax.jit`
(bitwise match of `aspect` to the eager value at `1e-12`); pytree round trip.

difference of the fully re-solved pipeline), CTH-like case at `ns = 25`, `ftol = 1e-14`,
two random directions on the `m <= 2, |n| <= 1` boundary modes of relative size `1e-3`:

| sum(bmnc[mid, 1:]^2) / bmnc[mid, 0]^2 | 1 | 7.226750e-02 | 1.179241e-03 | 1.179188e-03 | 4.5e-05 |

Open points:

- The mass profile is evaluated in NumPy from the input and is not differentiated; with `gamma != 0` it also uses the input `rbc(0, 0)` rather than the traced boundary.
- Profile types other than `power_series`, `two_power` and `line_segment` raise `NotImplementedError` in the output stage.
- `WoutArrays` carries the subset of `VmecWOut` listed above; `vp`, `beta_vol`, `buco`, `bvco`, `jcuru`, `jcurv`, `bsubsmns_full`, the currents and the `threed1` tables are not ported.

**<sub><sub>![P1 Badge](https://img.shields.io/badge/P1-orange?style=flat)</sub></sub>  Trace the boundary-dependent mass normalization**

When `gamma != 0` and callers differentiate or evaluate at a supplied boundary, the actual VMEC solve rebuilds the mass profile using that boundary's `rbc(0,0)`, while this output stage always reads the original template value. Thus perturbed calls return `pres`, `presf`, and `betatotal` inconsistent with the equilibrium that was solved, and their gradients omit this direct boundary dependence. Compute this factor from the traced boundary or reject adiabatic inputs rather than returning a wrong converged result.

PR #851 [CLOSED, not merged] Let Python attach attributes to VmecModel
################################################################################
author: jurasic-pf | opened: 2026-09-12 15:39 UTC | closed: 2026-09-12 15:43 UTC
branch: proximafusion:jurasic/vmecmodel-dynamic-attr -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/851
files (2): vmecpp/vmec/pybind11/pybind_vmec.cc +4 -1, tests/test_vmec_model_dynamic_attr.py (added) +43 -0

--- description ---
## Scope

Bug: `py::class_<VmecModel>` in `src/vmecpp/cpp/vmecpp/vmec/pybind11/pybind_vmec.cc` is
declared without `py::dynamic_attr()`. Python code that wants to attach state to a solved
model instance, for example a cached factorization or some metadata, cannot: `model.some_cache
= ...` raises `AttributeError`. Code that wraps such a setattr in a silent `try/except
AttributeError` hides the failure instead of surfacing it.

## Change

Add `py::dynamic_attr()` to the `VmecModel` class binding. This gives each `VmecModel`
instance a per-instance `__dict__`, the same mechanism pybind11 uses for any Python class:
one extra pointer in the instance layout, plus a dict allocated lazily the first time an
attribute is set. No other binding or method changes.

## Tests and measurements

Added `tests/test_vmec_model_dynamic_attr.py`:
- setting and reading back an arbitrary Python attribute on a `VmecModel` created from
  `examples/data/solovev.json`;
- setting an attribute, then calling `solve()`, and checking the attribute is unchanged and
  `get_state()` still returns a finite, correctly shaped array (dynamic attributes do not
  interfere with the C++-backed state).

Both tests pass locally. Full existing test suite unaffected (no other binding changed).

################################################################################
PR #857 [CLOSED, not merged] autodiff: block-tridiagonal assembly of H_SS^T for a direct adjoint solve
################################################################################
author: jurasic-pf | opened: 2026-09-12 16:01 UTC | closed: 2026-09-27 09:53 UTC
branch: proximafusion:jurasic/autodiff-direct-adjoint-solve -> jurasic/autodiff-state-layout
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/857
files (3): src/vmecpp/autodiff.py +221 -29, tests/test_autodiff_cache.py +1 -1, tests/test_autodiff_direct_adjoint.py (added) +220 -0

--- description ---
# autodiff: assemble H_SS^T as a block tridiagonal matrix for a direct adjoint solve

## Scope

The adjoint system `H_SS^T lambda = b` (the transposed force operator
restricted to the solved interior DOFs) was solved with preconditioned
GMRES, using `apply_preconditioner` as the preconditioner. The exact
Jacobian is a few percent asymmetric, and `apply_preconditioner` is VMEC's
descent preconditioner rather than an approximate inverse of the transposed
operator, so at realistic resolutions (~23k solved DOFs at `mpol=9, ntor=9,
ns=51`) GMRES needs on the order of `10^4` iterations, each a Hessian-vector
product through the exact force Jacobian.

## Change

- `test_force_depends_only_on_neighboring_surfaces` verifies, via direct
  Hessian-vector probes, that the force on surface `j` depends only on the
  state at surfaces `j - 1`, `j`, `j + 1` — for every span type
  (`r_cc`/`r_ss`/`z_sc`/`z_cs`/`lambda_sc`/`lambda_cs` in the 3D test case),
  restricted to `solved` rows (the fixed-boundary `r`/`z` entries at the last
  surface are not force rows in this sense and are excluded from the check).
- Added `assemble_block_tridiagonal_hessian(model, layout)`, which builds
  `H_SS^T` (restricted to `state_layout(model).solved`) as an exact
  `scipy.sparse.csc_matrix`, using Hessian-vector products with a 3-coloring
  of surfaces: for color `c` and mode `k`, one probe with ones at every solved
  entry at that mode on surfaces of that color recovers a whole slice of
  columns at once (no two solved entries of the same color and mode are
  adjacent, so their probed columns cannot collide in a tridiagonal operator's
  rows). A hit row on surface `i` is attributed to the probed column on
  surface `i - (((i - c + 1) % 3) - 1)`, the unique probed surface among
  `{i - 1, i, i + 1}` of color `c`.
- Added `_AdjointFactorCache`, keyed by `id(model)`, caching the
  `state_layout` and the `scipy.sparse.linalg.splu` factor of the assembled
  matrix alongside the model, so repeated cotangents through the same
  converged model reuse both the assembly and the factorization.
- `_implicit_boundary_vjp` gained `adjoint_solver: Literal["direct", "gmres"]
  = "direct"`. `"gmres"` is the unchanged, previously-existing preconditioned
  GMRES path (`_solve_adjoint_gmres`), kept for comparison and as a fallback.
  `"direct"` factorizes and solves via `_solve_adjoint_direct`.
- `make_solver`/`DifferentiableVmec` gained the same `adjoint_solver`
  parameter, defaulting to `"direct"`, threaded through `_backward_callback`.

## Tests and measurements

New file `tests/test_autodiff_direct_adjoint.py` (3D fixed-boundary,
`ncurr=0` case, same construction as `tests/test_autodiff.py`'s 3D fixture):

- `test_force_depends_only_on_neighboring_surfaces`: the locality claim the
  assembly depends on, checked directly via Hessian-vector probes at
  representative `solved` indices on every interior surface, for every
  present span.
- `test_assembled_matrix_reproduces_hessian_vector_products`: the assembled
  matrix times a random vector on `solved` reproduces
  `exact_hessian_vector_product_transpose` restricted to `solved`, to
  `rtol=1e-10`.
- `test_direct_solve_residual_is_small`: `splu` factor solve residual is
  `< 1e-8` relative.
- `test_direct_and_gmres_vjp_agree`: `_implicit_boundary_vjp` with
  `adjoint_solver="direct"` and `="gmres"` agree on a random cotangent to
  `rtol=1e-5`.
- `test_quasisymmetry_gradient_matches_between_solvers`: the end-to-end
  `jax.value_and_grad` gradient of a QS objective agrees between the two
  solvers to `rtol=1e-4`.
- `test_direct_is_the_default_adjoint_solver`,
  `test_adjoint_solver_must_be_a_known_literal`: defaults and validation.

All of `tests/test_autodiff.py`, `tests/test_autodiff_cache.py`,
`tests/test_autodiff_state_layout.py`,
`tests/test_autodiff_direct_adjoint.py`, `tests/test_geometry.py`, and
`tests/test_hessian.py` pass (34 passed, 1 skipped — Enzyme-gated) with
`adjoint_solver="direct"` as the new default, i.e. every existing gradient
test (`test_solve_vjp_is_the_transpose_of_the_forward_sensitivity`,
`test_quasisymmetry_gradient_through_a_three_dimensional_solve`, and the
GMRES-based forward-sensitivity cross-check) now exercises the direct solve
and still passes at unchanged tolerances.

`tests/test_autodiff_cache.py::test_on_failure_nan_warns_and_returns_nan_on_adjoint_failure`
was updated to accept `**kwargs` in its monkeypatched
`_implicit_boundary_vjp` stub, since the real function now takes
`adjoint_solver` as a keyword.

**Wall time**, single-core (`OMP_NUM_THREADS=1`):

CTH-like fixed-boundary, `ncurr=0` with the converged `ncurr=1` iota profile
prescribed as an 8th-order power series, `ns=25` (`mpol=5, ntor=4`,
2740 solved DOFs):

| step | direct | GMRES |
|---|---|---|
| assembly | 9.63 s | n/a |
| factorization | 0.07 s | n/a |
| per-cotangent solve | 1.2-1.7 ms (3 runs) | 16.1-16.6 s (3 runs) |

Same case at `ns=51`, `mpol=ntor=7` (14077 solved DOFs; GMRES skipped here —
at this DOF count and the `ns=25` per-iteration cost, a GMRES run alone would
likely exceed the ~10 minute budget for this measurement; the assembly+direct
numbers below already fit comfortably inside it):

| step | direct |
|---|---|
| forward solve (context, not part of the adjoint) | 5.5 s |
| assembly | 59.7 s |
| factorization | 1.6 s |
| per-cotangent solve | 18.7-19.6 ms (3 runs) |

At `ns=25` the one-time assembly+factorization cost (~9.7 s) already pays for
itself against a single GMRES solve (~16 s), and every additional cotangent
through the same converged model is a ~1 ms sparse solve rather than another
tens-of-seconds GMRES run. The assembly cost scales with the number of
`(color, mode)` probes (each one Hessian-vector product), which grows with
`mpol * (ntor + 1)` per span rather than with `ns`, so the `ns=51,
mpol=ntor=7` case's larger assembly time (~60 s) mostly reflects the larger
mode count, not the larger `ns`.

## Open issues

- `_AdjointFactorCache` is a module-level singleton keyed by `id(model)` with
  no eviction; a long-running process that creates many distinct converged
  models (e.g. one per boundary in a sweep, without going through
  `DifferentiableVmec`'s own `_ModelCache`) will accumulate factor cache
  entries for models that are no longer reachable from Python, since `id()`
  can be reused after a model is garbage collected. In practice
  `DifferentiableVmec`'s `_ModelCache` (branch 1) already bounds how many
  models are alive at once (default 4), and each cached model gets at most
  one factor entry, so combined use is bounded; a standalone user of
  `assemble_block_tridiagonal_hessian`/`_AdjointFactorCache` outside that path
  does not get the same bound automatically.
- `assemble_block_tridiagonal_hessian`'s per-probe cost is one
  `exact_hessian_vector_product_transpose` call over the full state size;
  this is already much cheaper in aggregate than one Hessian-vector product
  per solved DOF (the naive dense assembly), but has not been compared here
  against an assembly that batches multiple probes into fewer, larger C++
  calls, which could reduce Python-call overhead further at very large
  `mpol`/`ntor`.
- GMRES remains available (`adjoint_solver="gmres"`) but was not benchmarked
  at `ns=51, mpol=ntor=7` here for time; the `ns=25` numbers and the
  ~10^4-iteration count observed downstream at `mpol=9, ntor=9, ns=51` suggest it would
  be dramatically slower than the direct solve at that size, but this was not
  directly measured.

--- inline thread on src/vmecpp/autodiff.py:569-570 ---
> chatgpt-codex-connector[bot], 2026-09-12 16:05 UTC:
**<sub><sub>![P1 Badge](https://img.shields.io/badge/P1-orange?style=flat)</sub></sub>  Tie cached factors to the model lifetime**

When an optimizer evaluates more distinct boundaries than `_ModelCache` can retain, an evicted model can be collected while this module-level cache keeps only its integer ID, layout, and LU factor. Python can then reuse that ID for a new model, causing this lookup to return the old factor and silently compute the VJP using another boundary's Hessian; independently, every factor remains allocated indefinitely, so long optimization runs grow memory despite `cache_size`. Use model-identity-aware, eviction-bounded storage tied to the model cache rather than a bare `id(model)` key.

Useful? React with 👍 / 👎.

PR #867 [CLOSED, not merged] Accept only_coils with a zero pressure profile whatever pres_scale is
################################################################################
author: CharlesCNorton | opened: 2026-09-21 12:17 UTC | closed: 2026-09-25 21:41 UTC
branch: CharlesCNorton:only-coils-zero-pressure -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/867
files (2): vmecpp/common/vmec_indata/vmec_indata.cc +19 -7, vmecpp/common/vmec_indata/vmec_indata_test.cc +4 -0

--- description ---
**Change** - with `free_boundary_method = only_coils`, `IsConsistent` rejects an input when `pres_scale` is non-zero and `am` or `am_aux_f` has a non-zero entry, instead of whenever `pres_scale` is non-zero. The `curtor` check is unchanged and gets its own message.

**Cause** - the check stood in for a plasma without pressure with `pres_scale != 0`, while the pressure is `pres_scale` times the mass profile and `pres_scale` defaults to 1.0. An input with `am = [0]` and no `pres_scale` was rejected with "input variables 'curtor' and 'pres_scale' must be zero when 'free_boundary_method' is 'only_coils', but are 0 and 1", for a pressure that is zero.

**Evidence** - `cth_like_free_bdy` with `free_boundary_method = only_coils`, `curtor = 0`, `am = [0]` and no `pres_scale` was rejected with that message; it now converges in 630 iterations to the state of the same input with `pres_scale = 0`, the only spelling accepted before (identical residual history, MHD energy 4.961454e-02). `examples/data/w7x_free_bdy_vac.json`, with `am = [1, -1]` and `pres_scale = 0`, stays accepted.

**Tests** - `CheckOnlyCoilsRejectsCurrentAndPressure` in `vmec_indata_test` gains a zero mass profile with `pres_scale = 1`, which the previous check rejects; its current, non-zero pressure and all-zero cases are unchanged. `bazel test --config=opt //vmecpp/...` passes.

**Scope** - validation only.

PR #873 [CLOSED, not merged] Write the convergence traces of the wout to the HDF5 file
################################################################################
author: CharlesCNorton | opened: 2026-09-21 19:44 UTC | closed: 2026-09-25 22:00 UTC
branch: CharlesCNorton:wout-hdf5-convergence-trace -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/873
files (4): vmecpp/vmec/output_quantities/output_quantities.cc +16 -0, vmecpp/vmec/output_quantities/output_quantities.h +1 -1, vmecpp/vmec/output_quantities/output_quantities_io_test.cc +7 -0, vmecpp/vmec/output_quantities/test_helpers.h +8 -0

--- description ---
**Change** - `WOutFileContents::WriteTo` writes `itfsq`, `fsqt`, `force_residual_r`, `force_residual_z`, `force_residual_lambda`, `delbsq`, `restart_reason_timetrace` and `wdot`, and `LoadInto` reads them back through `READMEMBER_OPTIONAL`, so a file written before the change still loads, with empty traces. `itfsq` defaults to 0 for that case.

**Cause** - the traces were added to the struct, to the bindings and to `VmecWOut` in #282 and #328, and the HDF5 writer and reader were left as they were. `vmec_standalone` and `OutputQuantities::Save` therefore wrote an output file without the convergence history, and `OutputQuantities::Load` returned a wout that differs from the one that was saved. `OutputQuantitiesIO` compares every other struct with its defaulted `operator==` and the wout through `CheckWOutEquality`, whose list of members did not name these eight.

**Evidence** - `cth_like_fixed_bdy` through `run`, `save` and `load` on main: `itfsq` goes from 119 to an unset value and the seven arrays from 119 entries to none, while every other member comes back as saved. With the change the file holds the eight datasets under `/wout`, 119 entries each, and the loaded wout equals the saved one. A file written on main loads with the change, with `itfsq = 0` and the traces empty.

**Tests** - `CheckWOutEquality` names the eight members, and `OutputQuantitiesIO.WOut` also requires `wout == wout_fromfile`, which covers whatever the list does not name. On main the test fails on all eight.

**Scope** - the HDF5 file only; the NetCDF wout written through `VmecWOut.save` already carries these fields. The file grows by eight small datasets, seven of them one entry per iteration.

################################################################################

--- description ---
## Change

`bootstrap_current = true` replaces the enclosed toroidal current profile by the bootstrap current of the Redl closure during the iteration. Every 25 iterations, once the force residuals are below 1e-2, each thread evaluates the closure on its half surfaces from `bsupu`, `bsubu`, `bsupv`, `bsubv` and `gsqrt`: trapped fraction, `B_max`, `B_min`, `<B^2>`, `<1/B>`, and `<J.B>` with `G = bvcoH`, `I = bucoH`, `iota = chipH / phipH` and `psi_edge = -phi_edge / (2 pi)`. One thread integrates `(I/G)' = signgs mu0 <J.B> dVds / G^2` from the axis and writes the relaxed profile `currH + 0.5 (I_bs - currH)` back. `computeBContra` turns it into `chi'` at the next iteration. The run is converged when the force residuals are below `ftol` and the change in iota the closure last asked for, `max_j |I_bs - currH| / (|phip| <guu / sqrt(g)>)`, is below `bootstrap_tolerance` (default 1e-3). The profile carries across multigrid steps by linear interpolation.

`bootstrap_ne`, `bootstrap_te` and `bootstrap_ti` are power series in the normalized toroidal flux in 1e20 m^-3 and keV. They set the pressure `p = e (n_e T_e + n_i T_i)` with `n_i = n_e / bootstrap_zeff`; `am` and `pres_scale` are not read, and `ac` with `curtor` seed the current. `bootstrap_helicity_n` selects the quasi-symmetry, `N = helicity_n nfp`. The closure is the library `vmec/bootstrap_current`, the coupling is `IdealMhdModel::updateBootstrapCurrent`, the gate is in `Vmec::Evolve`. The fields are in `VmecINDATA`, JSON, HDF5, pybind and `VmecInput`; `examples/data/cth_like_bootstrap.json` is an input.

## Cause

With `ncurr = 1` the enclosed current is an input, so an equilibrium answers for a current the plasma may not carry. A stellarator at steady state carries the current its own density and temperature gradients drive. An outer loop around the solver costs a solve per iteration and breaks the derivative chain.

## Evidence

cth_like_fixed_bdy at `ftol = 1e-10` with `n_e = 0.02 (1 - 0.8 s) 1e20 m^-3` and `T_e = T_i = 0.65 (1 - 0.8 s) keV`, `p_0 = 417 Pa`:

| run | iterations | closure updates | net current | iota mid | iota edge |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| zero current, same pressure | 629 | | 0 A | 0.3366 | 0.3011 |
| closure, seed 0 A | 565 | 21 | 4601.2 A | 0.4082 | 0.3636 |
| closure, seed 3 kA two_power | 563 | | 4601.2 A | 0.4082 | 0.3636 |
| closure, hot restart | | | 4603.8 A | 0.4080 | 0.3636 |

The last requested change in iota is 3.8e-5. The current adds to the transform, as in a tokamak. On the converged wout, `mu0 <J.B> = signgs (G I' - I G') / V'` matches `jdotb` to 0.02% of `max |<J.B>|`. At the file's `ftol = 1e-6` the runs spread by 0.02 in edge iota; the zero-current run without the closure has edge iota 0.363 at 1e-6 and 0.301 at 1e-10, so `ftol` sets what `bootstrap_tolerance` resolves.

## Tests

- `bootstrap_current_test`: trapped fraction of `B = 1/(1 + eps cos theta)` against SIMSOPT's `compute_trapped_fraction` to 1e-6 for eps = 0.01, 0.1 and 0.3, and zero for a uniform field; Redl coefficients and `<J.B>` against SIMSOPT's `j_dot_B_Redl` to 1e-10 on three surfaces for helicity 0 and 1; the current integration inverts the identity to 1e-6; the kinetic pressure.
- `vmec_test`: `BootstrapClosureConvergesAndRaisesIota` checks convergence, update count, mismatch, the rise of `|iota|`, seed independence and the identity on the wout; `BootstrapClosureInputIsValidated` checks `am` empty, `ncurr = 1` and positive kinetic profiles on [0, 1].
- `bazel test --config=opt //vmecpp/...`, the CMake build with the Python extension, the fields through JSON and `VmecINDATA` with `vmecpp.run` and a hot restart, and pre-commit.

## Scope

The closure is Redl's fit for tokamaks and quasi-symmetric fields, evaluated in VMEC coordinates: the trapped fraction is a flux-surface average and `G`, `I` are the angle averages of `B_zeta`, `B_theta`. It is skipped with a warning while a surface has `|iota - N|` below 1e-3. `gamma` must be 0 and `bloat` 1. Following commits on this branch add the closure's response to the exact-derivative path and the Shaing-Callen closure of BOOTSJ for general stellarators with its Boozer transform.

--- comment by jurasic-pf, 2026-09-22 17:27 UTC ---
I think this is out of scope for `vmecpp`.
The formula only holds in the limit of quasisymmetry afaik, so including the calculation in a code that is supposed to be a general equilibrium solve is breaking single responsibility principle.

I agree that we can make it easier to include selfconsistent bootstrap calculation as part of the solve, by exposing more of the solver internals to the user, but I would suggest to either enable that codepath by the callback or use simsopt + the Python API for driving the solve.

PR #896 [CLOSED, not merged] Keep lamscale when ns_array repeats an entry
################################################################################
author: CharlesCNorton | opened: 2026-09-26 17:20 UTC | closed: 2026-09-26 17:31 UTC
branch: CharlesCNorton:repeated-ns-lamscale -> main
url: https://github.com/proximafusion/vmecpp/pull/896
files (4): vmecpp/vmec/pybind11/pybind_vmec.cc +0 -9, vmecpp/vmec/vmec/vmec.cc +5 -2, tests/test_init.py +23 -0, tests/test_iteration.py +5 -5

--- description ---
**Change** - `Vmec::InitializeRadial` resets `constants_` (`rmsPhiP` and `lamscale`) in the branch that re-evaluates the radial profiles, where `Vmec::run` reset them at the start of every multigrid step. `VmecModel.reinitialize()` and `refine_to()` drop their own resets, which this makes redundant.

**Cause** - `IsConsistent` allows `ns_array` to repeat an entry, and `InitializeRadial` skips that branch for a step whose `ns` equals the previous one, so such a step ran with `lamscale = 0`. Lambda dropped out of B, the lambda force norm divided by zero and `fsql` was NaN at the first iteration; the solver took that for a bad Jacobian, replaced the converged state with an axis re-guess and stopped with `The solver failed during the first iterations`.

**Evidence** - solovev, `ftol_array = [1e-8, 1e-12]`:

| `ns_array` | main | here |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `[11]`, `ftol 1e-12` | converges at iteration 183 | same iterations and residuals |
| `[11, 21]` | converges at 117, then 147 | same iterations and residuals |
| `[11, 11]` | converges at 117; the second step starts with `lamscale = 0` and `fsql = NaN`, and the run fails | converges at 117, then 67 from the first step's residuals |

The `[11, 11]` result agrees with the single `[11]` step to 2e-9 in `rmnc` and 4e-10 in `zmns`.

**Tests** - `test_repeated_ns_array_entry_resumes_the_previous_step` in `tests/test_init.py` runs `[11, 11]` against a single `[11]` step; on main it fails with that error.

**Scope** - a step that changes `ns` resets the constants as before.
